# Module map

How the server is assembled and how modules connect. **Update this file in the same change that adds a module, event type, or cross-module dependency.**

## Kernel (spec 93 — `radd/kernel/`)

The generic machinery every plugin builds on; it never disables. `kernel/plugin.py` holds the contract (`RaddPlugin`, a manifest of contributions), `kernel/registry.py` one registry per contribution kind (each read blind by exactly one generic consumer), `kernel/loader.py` loading plus the `depends_on`/`api_version` checks, `kernel/specs.py` the pure contribution specs (the kernel imports nothing from `radd.modules.*`), and `kernel/sockets.py` the typed plugin-to-plugin sockets. The design and every socket's contract: `docs/plugin-platform.md` §1, §4 and §4a. Core invariants: `tests/test_kernel.py`.

- **The manifest REFUSES a bare value where a tuple is declared (RADD-756).** `on_startup=_startup` instead of `(_startup,)` is stored happily and fails wherever the field is finally iterated — it shipped once as an image that could not finish `lifespan` behind a green suite — and `depends_on="items"` fails SILENTLY as five one-character plugin names. `__post_init__` raises for every tuple field, derived from the annotations; `tests/test_app_startup.py` runs the real `create_app` lifespan.
- **A runtime reach into an optional plugin** is a socket, a contributed spec, or `"<plugin id>" in registries.plugins` — never `except ImportError` or `settings.modules`, which cannot see a runtime disable (CLAUDE.md rule 1).
- **`ContributionSource` (RADD-748)** records which plugin registered a contribution beside the spec, not on it: only the registry knows, and unregister drops the source with the spec.

## Assembly

`radd.app:create_app()` reads `settings.modules` (ordered dotted paths), imports each package's `plugin: RaddPlugin`, verifies `depends_on`, then:

- mounts every router under `/api/v1`
- registers module `exception_handlers`
- runs module `on_startup` hooks in the lifespan
- applies `openapi_augmentors` on every OpenAPI render (this is how dynamic schema stays live)

`RaddPlugin` (see `radd/kernel/plugin.py`) is the plugin contract. Alembic discovers models the same way (`import_models(settings.modules)`), so a module's tables migrate iff the module is enabled.

The rest of the platform — `/capabilities` and the backend-assembled UI manifest (chokepoints 2 and 3), automation triggers from the event-type registry (chokepoint 1, pinned by `tests/test_trigger_registry.py`), frontend federation and the slot vocabulary, entity auto-wiring (`kernel/entities.py`; `milestones` is the north-star plugin), plugin-contributable RBAC registries, and the plugin lifecycle with per-owner admission (`kernel/admission.py`, `kernel/runtime.py`) and managed packages ([spec 126](specs/126-external-plugin-workflow.md), [spec 127](specs/127-managed-plugin-packages.md)) — is described in `docs/plugin-platform.md`, `docs/plugin-ui.md` and `docs/plugin-development.md`.

App-level pieces outside the module system: `radd/middleware.py` (`CommitBeforeSendMiddleware` buffers each response until the request's session has committed — FastAPI's yield-dependency teardown otherwise sends before commit; `text/event-stream` passes through) and SPA serving (`_mount_spa` in `app.py`, `RADD_WEB_DIST` overrides). Shared core utils: `radd/smtp.py` (the synchronous SMTP wire helper; `html_body` makes `multipart/alternative` with the text part FIRST — the sender rows reach it through mailintake), `radd/mailrender.py` (pure mail composition: no session, no module import, no settings read; everything interpolated is `html.escape`d and markdown is NOT rendered; the canonical `issue_url`/`page_url`/`inbox_url`/`preferences_url` and `unsubscribe_headers`), `radd/tasklists.py` (ticks one GFM task box and refuses with `TaskToggleConflict` when the stored body is not the one the reader rendered — items and comments have no version column) and `radd/worker.py` (`PeriodicLoop`: tick, log-don't-crash, sleep, restart-safe stop).

**Module-internal package splits (the barrel is the boundary).** When a module's `service.py` outgrows the 300-line rule it becomes a `service/` PACKAGE of per-concern modules stacked bottom-up with no cycles, and the package `__init__` is a barrel that re-exports **only the module's public surface** — never the `_`-prefixed helpers, whose privacy is the whole point of rule 1. `items/service/` is the reference: `visibility`/`queries` (leaves) → `relations`/`read`/`listing` → `links` → `core`, barrel = the same 25 functions the former god-file exposed. An intra-module caller that genuinely needs a helper imports the concern module directly (`from .service.visibility import _internal_visible` in `items/bulk.py`); a caller in ANOTHER module does not get one — it asks for a public function.

SLQ amendments live in their specs and CLAUDE.md "Query language (SLQ)": `epic` is reflexive — an epic belongs to itself, one rule in `items/hierarchy.py::nearest_epic_case` (as a strict ancestor an epic matched no `epic.*` condition, not even a negated one, because `NOT (NULL IN (…))` is NULL); the timesheet groups by epic through `items.service.epics_for_items`; relational fields `logged_by`/`commented_by` (spec 97); the worklog dialect (spec 98).

**In-transaction hooks** (`radd/hooks.py`): a module can subscribe to another module's mutation and react *inside the same transaction* (`@hooks.on(ProjectEvent.PROJECT_CREATED)`). Use only when atomicity with the trigger is required (e.g. workflow seeds default states with the project); everything else belongs to outbox consumers, which run after commit.

## Modules

| Module | Purpose | Depends on | Emits events | Extension points used |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `events` | Transactional outbox **and durable audit log** (append-only, never pruned, every row `actor_id`-attributed): `events` table, `emit` inside the caller's txn, `GET /events?after=` for consumers (`global.manage` since RADD-1213; spec 86 dropped `workspace_id` from events + the delivery predicate — the realtime frame is authenticated-only). Filtered reads added for audit/history: `query_events` (the admin audit query) and `entity_activity(entity_type, entity_id, related_event_types)` (an item's full feed — direct events UNION related events whose payload `item_id` matches). Indexes `ix_events_entity` (entity_type, entity_id) + `ix_events_actor_id`. **`runner.py` (consolidation): `run_head_seeded(consumer_name, *, batch_size, plan, deliver) -> int`** — THE shared scaffold for delivery-flavored outbox consumers: no offset row → seed at the stream head + commit + return 0 (never replay the backlog at a channel/recipient); read a batch; per-event `plan(session, event)` with log-don't-crash (planning MAY write rows — csat does); cursor + planning writes COMMIT BEFORE `deliver(plans)` runs post-commit (at-most-once). Used by the csat sender, the googlechat notifier, and mailintake outbound. The semantically-divergent consumers stay hand-rolled: search indexer (backlog replay IS the index build, savepoint per event), notify (watch-only bootstrap over the backlog), automations engine (one txn per event), webhooks (fan-out rows are the delivery). **Quiet scope (spec 100): `events.quiet` + the `events.silent` column.** A `ContextVar` (`quiet.py`, re-exported from `service`) that `emit` reads, so a bulk importer marks its whole event stream without any service in the call chain (items/comments/worklogs) learning that an import is running — and because a ContextVar is copied into tasks created inside the scope, ONE `with` around a background run covers every write it makes. Consumers that reach OUTSIDE the instance skip silent rows: notify, webhooks fan-out, the automations engine (`should_process`), the realtime broadcaster, and everything on `run_head_seeded`. Consumers that build INTERNAL state deliberately do NOT: the search index (an imported issue must be findable) and `query_events`/`entity_activity` (it must have history). Each skips **inside its loop and still advances the cursor** — filtering silent rows out in SQL would make an all-silent batch read as an empty stream and wedge the cursor. Preferred over advancing consumer offsets past an import's event range, which would also swallow unrelated concurrent activity. Tests: `tests/test_quiet_events.py`. **THE PAYLOAD CONTRACT (RADD-922): every ITEM-SCOPED event carries `payload["item"]`** — one ref (`items.service.item_ref` / `ref_from`) with `id`, `key` (`TD-42`), `title`, `kind`, `priority`, `state{id,name,category}`, `project{id,key,name}`, `team{id,name}|null`, `assignee{id,name}|null`; the ITEM events nest their whole `ItemRead` there instead, and `changes` stays at the ROOT because it describes the event, not the item. People are `{id,name,email}` refs via `auth.service.user_ref` (`author` on comments/worklogs, `voter`/`requester` on approvals), never bare ids — a payload exists to be READ, and `assignee_id` alone renders a uuid at someone and calls it a notification. **Before this the stream had fourteen shapes**: the key was `key` on item/csat events, `item_key` on sla/page_link, and ABSENT from the other nine (comments, worklogs, attachments, vcs, weblinks, approvals, participants, watchers); `project_id` was on two families; `item_id` was on everything except the item events, which carried `id`. The cost was in the consumers — `notify/consumer.py` resolved the key FOUR ways (two payload spellings + two DB round trips), `googlechat/formatter.py` branched on the spelling, `search/indexer.py` opened with a silent `if "project_id" not in payload: return`, and `gates.state_category_is` read a key NOTHING emitted (dead: always false). Enforced structurally by `tests/test_event_payloads.py`, which AST-parses every `emit()` call — resolving payloads built as a local or a helper — and fails an item-scoped emitter that does not build the ref, or one that reintroduces `item_key`/`item_id`. Migration `d922payload` DELETES the pre-change rows rather than shipping a compat reader (owner decision): the stream is append-only, so History/audit/cycle-reporting start empty; the search index is unaffected (own table + startup sweep). **RADD-923 — the contribution seam**: an emitter now passes `subjects={"item": id}` and the KERNEL writes the ref (`registries.entity_refs`, an `EntityRefSpec` per entity type; `items`, `projects` and every auto-wired `EntitySpec` register one). You cannot forget to build a ref you never build, which is why eleven emitters stopped importing `items` to describe an item. `EventTypeSpec.subjects` declares what an event is ABOUT and the LOADER refuses to boot a plugin naming a subject nothing describes; `EventTypeSpec.payload_schema` declares only the REMAINDER (the kernel wrote the refs, so deriving beats declaring) and is served beside the samples, which is the half sampling cannot answer — an event that has never fired still shows its shape. `Packet` carries `subjects` per entity type (`item_ids` is a property), and `apply_event` seeds EVERY ref the event names, so a non-item event is first-class. **Contributed ACTIONS execute**: `AutomationNodeSpec` gains `subject` + `apply`, and the executor dispatches a registered spec before the `ActionType` enum — previously it raised, logged "unknown action type" and dropped the node, so a plugin could contribute a trigger and a gate but never an action. `plan` runs on every walk (free, identical dry run); `apply` runs inside the executor's SAVEPOINT, its `RunBudget` and its `events.automated()` scope, so a third-party action cannot spin the engine, escape the budget or take a branch down. Its declared atom is enforced where the automation is WRITTEN, like `act_as`. The SPA generates a form from `params_schema` (`SchemaForm`) — the promise the spec made and nothing kept. North-star: `milestones/automation.py` contributes an action on its OWN entity, driven by its OWN auto-wired event, with zero edits to `automations`, the kernel or the SPA (`tests/test_event_subjects.py`, `web/scripts/plugin-contribution-proof.mjs`). The identity sequence is NOT restarted — restarting it would put new events below the stored consumer offsets and every worker would skip them in silence. **Spec 123 — the LEDGER columns:** `project_id` (from the subject refs), `entity_label` (the entity's display label at write time — outlives the row), `search_text` (event words + label + changed fields and values; trigram GIN, guarded on `pg_trgm`) + a GIN over `payload -> 'changes'` (`d123ledger`, SQL-backfilled). `emit(changes=)` writes the `kernel.changes` diff; an event whose `EventTypeSpec.has_changes` is True and carries none raises `ChangesRequired`; `emit` also adds the event's own entity as a subject when a ref is registered. `EventTypeSpec.audited=False` marks noise (`mail.failed`, `notification.created`, `automation.scheduled`). `query_events` gained `project_id`, `entity_type` (comma list), `changed_field` (containment), `source` (`EventSource`: people/automations/system), `exclude_event_types`; `q` matches `search_text` only. `ledger.py` is the pure derivation. | — (router imports `auth.deps`/`auth.authz` for enforcement — the one tolerated backward edge) | — | — |
| `projects` | **Spec 86 stage 3 FINISHED the workspace eradication:** the `workspace` module was renamed to `projects` and the `workspaces` + `workspace_memberships` tables were DROPPED (destructive migration `f4a9c31e77d2`). No workspace entity, singleton resolver, or `default_workspace` accessor survives — projects are global containers with globally-unique keys. `service.create_project`/`get_project`/`list_projects` only; `POST /projects` takes no `workspace_id`, `GET /projects` lists ALL projects. `ProjectRead.permissions` = the CURRENT user's effective permissions (hydrated via `authz.permissions_for_projects`, one batched pass). Global builtin roles + default work categories seed via on-startup ensures (auth `subscribers.ensure_seeded`, timelogging `categories.ensure_seeded`) — replacing the retired `workspace.created` hook. `types.py` exposes `ProjectEvent.PROJECT_CREATED` + `ProjectEntity.PROJECT` (`WorkspaceEvent`/`WORKSPACE_CREATED` gone). **RADD-1009 — rename + describe:** `projects.description` (Text NOT NULL '' — migration `d1009projdesc`), `ProjectUpdate {name?, description?}` (the KEY is deliberately not a field: item keys derive from it), `service.update_project` (emits `project.updated` with a `changes` diff; a no-op PATCH emits nothing), `PATCH /projects/{id}` gated on `project.manage` for that project, `ProjectRead.description`, and the `update_project` MCP tool (kernel-enforced, `project_param`). SPA: `ProjectIdentityCard` at the top of Settings → Project → General; the description shows under the name on the projects index and the settings header. **RADD-1174 — deletion, owned here:** `service.delete_project` runs three layers inside one transaction — `inspect_project` (dispatches the in-txn hook **`project.inspecting`** with a mutable `ProjectInspection{counts, blockers}`; a blocker is a 409 naming it), the hook **`project.deleting`** (`ProjectDeleting{project, actor_id}` — owners remove what the DB cannot cascade), then the RADD-892 `ProjectPurgeSpec` registry in FK-safe order (`purge_project_rows`, the loop jiraimport's rollback used to own) and the row. `project.deleted` is emitted BEFORE the delete so the subject ref resolves; its payload carries `key`, `name`, `removed` (the counts). Subscribers (`<module>/subscribers.py`, registered at import like the seeding hooks): **comments** (count + delete on the project's items — polymorphic parent, no FK), **attachments** (rows + grants in-txn, bytes best-effort after; the orphan GC keys on `item.deleted`, which a purge never emits), **views** (grants on the project's views), **fields** and **linktypes** (definitions scoped ONLY to this project are DELETED — with their scope rows cascaded away they would become GLOBAL, since no rows = every project; link types bypass `delete_type`'s usage guard because the project's own links are still present at hook time), **settings** (`scoped_settings` at project scope — bare `scope_id`), **notify** (project subscriptions), **items** (count + detaches children moved to OTHER projects: `parent_id` has no ON DELETE action), **releases**/**forms**/**timelogging** (counts; worklogs + seconds over both anchors), **mailintake** (BLOCKS when a source's `default_project_id` or a rule's `project_id` is this project — with the default nulled `intake.accept` raises on every message and the webhook 5xxs, so refusing up front beats degrading). `GET /projects/{id}/content` (the inspection) + `DELETE /projects/{id}` (204), both gated on the new GLOBAL atom **`project.delete`** (auth-owned beside `project.create`, `implied_by=global.manage`; global on purpose — `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` feeds the builtin project Admin role and a delegated admin must not be able to destroy the project). MCP `delete_project` (kernel-enforced on the atom, no `project_param`). jiraimport's rollback now calls `delete_project` instead of its private purge loop. SPA: `DeleteProjectCard` (Danger zone, last on General, shown only to holders of the atom) → dialog with the server's counts, the blockers linked to Settings → Email, and the KEY typed back before the danger button enables. Tests: `tests/test_project_delete.py`. | events | `project.created`, `project.updated`, `project.deleted` | dispatches the in-txn `project.created` hook (workflow/views/itemtypes seed per-project defaults; builtin roles + work categories seed at startup instead) and the `project.inspecting` / `project.deleting` teardown hooks (twelve subscribers, listed left) |
| `projects` (spec 50 additions) | **RADD-1389: `GET /instance/status` is DELETED** — its typed schema named sso/ldap/mailintake/ai from a core module; Settings → Server status now renders `GET /capabilities` generically (one row per non-connector capability with its `summary`, linked through the owning plugin's settings page; connectors fold into one row). Historically, `GET /instance/status` (instance-admin only) returns non-secret deploy flags (SSO/LDAP/SMTP/MFA/AI/storage/workers + connectors) for the Instance settings surface; `GET /instance` now resolves `work_week_days` through the scalar cascade (instance override wins over env) via a deferred import of `settings.service`; spec-67 follow-up: it (and the unauthenticated `/instance/login-options` mirror — same shape, harmless non-secrets) also carries **`timelog_hours_per_day`** (int, the global scalar via the same resolve) + **`timelog_days_per_week`** (int, env-only) so the SPA duration formatter stops hardcoding 8. | settings (deferred) | — | — |
| `settings` | **Two-scope** scalar settings cascade (specs 50/67 — spec 67 retired the workspace layer; a single-workspace deployment only duplicated the instance one): `scoped_settings` table (`scope` ∈ instance/project, nullable `scope_id` = project id or NULL for instance, `key`, JSONB `value`); a typed `SettingKey` alias + the kernel `SettingSpec` **registry** (RADD-891 — see the addendum row below: each owning plugin contributes `settings_keys=(SettingSpec(...),)`) — a key opts into the cascade by being registered on the module that reads it, its ultimate default = the matching `config.Settings` env attr (`SettingSpec.default`, dynamic `getattr` dispatch — now a kernel property since kernel purity does not forbid `radd.config`), `SettingSpec.scopes` declares where it may be SET (most keys both scopes; `timelog_hours_per_day` is INSTANCE-ONLY), and **`SettingSpec.choices`** (spec 107 cleanup) enumerates a STRING key's accepted values — writes outside the set 409 in `_coerce` (a free-typed "gaurds" must never reach a StrEnum coercion at read time), `list_for_scope` serves `choices`, and the generic `ScopedSettingsEditor` renders a SELECT with friendly labels (`lib/meta.SETTING_CHOICE_LABELS`) instead of a free-text input — `workflow_transition_mode` is the first adopter. `service.resolve(key, project_id?)` returns the project override else the instance override else the env default — the lookup order is filtered to the spec's scopes, so a stale narrower row can never shadow an instance-only key; `set_value`/`clear_value`/`list_for_scope` (workspace scope now 422s at the enum; a write at a scope outside the spec's `scopes` 409s). `PUT/GET/DELETE /scoped-settings` gated per scope (instance-admin / project.manage). Registered keys (now declared where they are READ, not here): `work_week_days` (SLA resolves it per item project; `GET /instance` at instance scope via a deferred import — the one workspace→settings request-time edge), **`timelog_hours_per_day`** (INT, **instance-only** since the spec-67 follow-up — one global `1d`↔seconds factor for every duration parse/format: worklogs, estimates, timesheets, cycle handles; exposed on `GET /instance` for the SPA formatter; migration `c4f8a25d91e7` defensively dropped project-scope rows), **`workflow_transition_mode`** (STRING off\|guards\|strict, spec 61 — `workflow.check_transition` resolves it per item project), **`csat_enabled`** (BOOL, spec 65 — the csat sender resolves it per item project; config default `csat_enabled = False`, so surveys are per-project OPT-IN), **`estimation_points`** (BOOL, spec 70 — config default `False`; gates every story-points surface per project), and the four **directory keys** (spec 85, ALL instance-only, edited on Settings → Directory — the General tab's editor filters them out): `ldap_user_sync_base` (STRING — its env default is the pre-existing `RADD_LDAP_USER_SEARCH_BASE`, wired via the `SettingSpec.config_attr` override for keys whose default lives under a different config attr), `ldap_user_sync_enabled` + `ldap_user_sync_deactivate_missing` (BOOL, default False), `ldap_group_search_base` (STRING). Empty base-DN values fall back to `ldap.service.base_dn` at USE time (in the ldap module's resolvers), so the registered default stays the verbatim env value. **`GET /scoped-settings/resolve?key=&project_id=`** (spec 70) returns one key's cascade-RESOLVED value to ANY member (item.read on the project when given) — the SPA's `usePointsEnabled(projectId)` gate reads it, unlike the manage-gated scope editors. Frontend: workspace-settings **General** tab = the instance-scope editor (product defaults, admin-only), each project's Settings → General = its overrides; the **Server** tab (was "Instance") is deploy status only. Collections (fields/SLA/labels) stay ADDITIVE, not here. Migration `a7c2e19b4f30` promoted workspace rows to instance (one per key, newest wins) and dropped the rest. No events yet (admin-low-frequency). **RADD-891 — settings hosts the cascade mechanism, it no longer owns the keys.** Same shape as RADD-890's fix for `auth.types.Permission`, one registry over: `settings_keys=(SettingSpec…),)` on `RaddPlugin` existed since spec 93 and was read by nothing; now every feature's tunables live on the manifest of the module that resolves them, and `settings` validates against the kernel registry instead of hardcoding every key. `SettingKey` SURVIVES as the typed alias enum (ratcheted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, `settings` itself declares zero keys) for the same import-order reason `Permission` did — it types a FastAPI query param and a pydantic field at `settings`'s own fixed, early position in `RADD_MODULES`, long before most owning plugins (`ai`, `ldap`, `releases`, `timelogging`, `workflow`, `items`, `csat`) have registered theirs. Unlike `Permission`, nothing computes eagerly from the full key set at `settings`' import time (no equivalent of `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS`), so there is no static per-key scope/type table to keep in sync — `setting_spec(key)` reads type/scopes/prose/default LIVE off `radd.kernel.registries.settings` on every call. See the settings addendum row below for who owns what. | projects, auth | — | — **RADD-1390:** a row carries `homed` (its scope ∈ the spec's `page_scopes`, declared by the owning plugin); General pages render `!homed` — the host's `INSTANCE_/PROJECT_HOMED_SECTIONS` lists are gone. |
| `auth` | Users, session-cookie login (`radd_session`), personal access tokens (`radd_pat_…`, hash-only storage). **Spec 86 — the authz semantics:** there are no membership rows — every ACTIVE user holds the global member floor (`effective_permissions`/`permissions_for_projects` synthesize the role: inactive → nothing anywhere; instance_role=admin → ALL; any other active user → the member global set + per-project role grants). No `workspace_id` kwarg on the seams; instance admin is `users.instance_role` (`principals.is_instance_admin`, credential-aware). The `/workspaces/{id}/members` endpoints are DELETED — **`users.instance_role` (admin\|member) is THE role ladder**, managed via `PATCH /users/{id}` (instance admin; self-demotion 409); the `workspace_memberships` table + its service functions are GONE (SSO/LDAP now set `users.instance_role` directly). `/auth/me` emits a FLAT shape: top-level `global_role` (admin\|member) + the global-scope `permissions` union (`MeRead`). **Roles as data** (spec 06, `auth/roles.py` + `roles_router.py`): `roles` table (the builtin roles — Viewer ⊂ Member ⊂ Manager, Baseline, Requester, Public, Contributor — are GLOBAL rows, ensured on startup via `subscribers.ensure_seeded`, + custom permission sets; `GET/POST /roles` are global, key globally unique `uq_roles_key`), `GET /permissions` catalog (`{key, description, scope, resource, action}` from the permission registry (`PERMISSION_SCOPES` + `permission_parts`) — spec 50 CRUD atoms feed the resource×action roles matrix; **scope wire values `project`\|`global`\|`instance` — renamed the middle scope from "workspace"** [scope-language only: atom KEYS like `workspace.manage` are unchanged — its resource IS the workspace entity — and the authz helper is now `global_scope_permissions`]), direct project membership as a project-scoped role grant (RADD-929 — `GET /role-grants?project_id=` reads it, `POST`/`DELETE /role-grants` write it; the `project_members` table and its `/projects/{id}/members` endpoints are gone); exports `CurrentUser`/`OptionalUser` deps (`auth/deps.py`) and the RBAC seam `auth/authz.py`: `Permission` enum (defined in `auth/types.py`, re-exported), `effective_permissions` union engine, `require`, `permissions_for_projects`, `subjects_for` → `{role_ids, team_ids}` (feeds the fields grant checks; unit-tested in `server/tests/test_authz.py` — `uv run pytest`). The pre-07 ladder shim (`effective_project_role`/`permissions_to_role`/`role_at_least`) is GONE — spec 07 removed its last consumer (fields). **Spec 75:** `dashboard` registered in CRUD_RESOURCES (GLOBAL scope, umbrella `global.manage` directly — the member/issue_type precedent) → `dashboard.create/update/delete` atoms; `dashboard.create` doubles as the dashboard-sharing broadcast gate; the builtin admin role carries the triple as global-scoped riders (display-only — global checks key on the member floor; migration `9176930be113` mirrors them into stored rows). **Spec 84 (user administration):** `users.source` (`UserSource` local\|ldap\|oidc\|unknown; set at every creation point — local create/seed, `ldap.provision`, `sso.provision`; migration `8dcb99d261e6` backfills local where a password hash exists, else unknown, and `unknown` is claimed by the next directory login) + `users.last_login_at` (stamped in `create_session` — the ONE seam every login path mints sessions through). `GET /users` grows `q`/`source`/`active` filters (spec 86: the workspace-name summary column is gone); `PATCH /users/{id}` (instance admin): `name` + `active` + **`instance_role` (spec 86 — the Users page's role dropdown now writes here)** — deactivation REVOKES sessions and blocks all login paths (every auth lookup checks `User.active`), self-deactivation AND self-demotion 409; `GET /users/duplicates` (instance admin): pure heuristic `find_duplicate_groups` — shared lowercased email LOCAL PART or exact case-insensitive name, inactive included/flagged, identical groups deduped — feeds the merge UI over the existing `POST /users/{id}/merge`. **Spec 87 — global-scope grants became deliverable + dead atoms removed:** `global_role_grants` (`auth/grants.py`; one row = one role held instance-wide by a user or a team, exactly one of user_id/team_id, CHECK-constrained) is THE delivery mechanism for global-scope atoms — before it, `global_scope_permissions` read `users.instance_role` alone, so all ~47 global atoms in the roles matrix (label.create, team.update, sla.*, cycle.*, …) were ungrantable to a non-admin and the matrix advertised checkboxes that granted nothing. Grants apply at BOTH scopes: `global_scope_permissions(role, permission_sets)` unions them in, and `_granted_role_ids` adds them on every project (so project atoms inside a globally-granted role are live too) — hence `permissions_for_projects` and `subjects_for` pick them up for free. `GET/PUT /roles/{id}/global-grants` (PUT gated on `role.update`, full-state replace; a grant RESTRICTs role deletion). `/auth/me` now returns `effective_permissions` (so grants show up) + `manages_teams` (per-team delegation is invisible to the permission union — it gates the SPA's Teams nav). **Atoms DROPPED** (migration `3a363a121502` strips them from stored role JSONB — `RoleRead.permissions` is `list[Permission]`, so a stale string would 500 `GET /roles`): `user.delete` + `import.run` (no endpoint could ever exist — accounts are deactivated/merged, the importer is a CLI script) and `dashboard.update`/`dashboard.delete` (dashboards decide by spec-57 ownership; no atom was ever consulted) — the `user`/`dashboard` ResourceSpecs carry explicit `actions=` tuples now. `PATCH /users/{id}` is gated on `user.update` with `instance_role` changes kept behind a hard instance-admin check (otherwise the atom would be a self-serve route to admin). **Spec 89 — hard delete with reassignment (REVERSES spec 87's removal of `user.delete`):** `GET /users/{id}/content` (`user_content_summary` — issues/comments/docs/views/dashboards/teams/attachments/approvals + worklog count & seconds) then `DELETE /users/{id}?reassign_to=…`. The row REALLY goes (cf. merge, which keeps a deactivated shell). Authored work repoints to the successor — REQUIRED when the account owns anything (409), omitted when it owns nothing; **worklogs are DELETED not moved** (crediting someone with hours they never worked would corrupt every timesheet). **RADD-784: ACCESS DIES WITH THE ACCOUNT** — `_DELETE_WITH_ACCOUNT` destroys project/team/group memberships, `team_managers`, user role grants, form shares, participations, watches, stars and the inbox instead of the old merge-dedupe transfer (which silently added the successor to every project the leaver was in, at the leaver's role); user-subject `access_grants` are deleted, never repointed; `teams.owner_id` is EXCLUDED from the delete repoint (SET NULL — a new owner is chosen deliberately, `owned_teams` no longer forces a successor). The flip side is the **viability gate**: `successor_viability` compares EFFECTIVE permissions per scope (global + every project via 2 batched `permissions_for_projects` calls + granted wiki spaces), lattice-aware (`item.read` covers `item.read@own`), and the delete 409s naming what is missing; `GET /users/{id}/successor-check?candidate_id=` is the dialog's preview (`SuccessorCheck{viable, gaps[{scope_type,label,scope_id,missing}]}`); an admin leaver needs an admin successor. `tests/test_successor_viability.py`. Guards (`ensure_deletable`, pure): no self-delete, successor must be active and not the deletee. Deleting forced a fix to the merge machinery: `approval_requests.requested_by`, `approval_votes.user_id`, `pages.updated_by` (all FK-blocking) and `teams.owner_id` (SET NULL → would orphan owned teams) were MISSING from `_MERGE_REPOINT` — a latent merge bug too. Columns that reference users with NO FK (`events.actor_id`, `notifications.*`, `item_watchers`, `attachments.created_by`) would silently dangle, so they are repointed with a successor and purged/nulled without one. `tests/test_user_delete.py` asserts repoint completeness against the LIVE schema, so a new user-referencing table cannot quietly break deletion. Emits `user.deleted` carrying email/name/successor/moved-counts — once the row is gone that event IS the record. **Spec 91 — grants became scopeable (project OR global) + a unified Grant Role dialog:** `global_role_grants` gained a nullable **`project_id`** (NULL = global, unchanged; set = the role held only on that project) — one table, one resolution path. `grants.granted_role_ids(user_id, project_id=None)` returns global-only at global scope, global ∪ this-project at a project; `project_granted_role_ids(user_id, project_ids)` feeds the batch `permissions_for_projects`; `_granted_role_ids` now passes `project.id`. Grant-centric CRUD (`create_grant`/`delete_grant`/`grants_for_subject`) behind `POST /role-grants {role_id, user_id|team_id, project_ids[]}` (empty = one global grant, else one per project) + `DELETE /role-grants/{id}` + `GET /role-grants?team_id=|user_id=` (gated role.update for writes, item.read for reads). `replace_grants` (the Roles page's per-role global editor) now scoped to `project_id IS NULL`. Migration `389293e3d799` adds the column + swaps the unique constraints to include project_id (partial-null → global dups guarded in code). `tests/test_global_grants.py` proves a project-scoped grant is live ONLY on its project. What every active user holds without being granted anything is now the builtin **`baseline`** role (`BuiltinRoleKey.BASELINE`, seeded `item.read@own` since RADD-825), read by `authz.baseline_permissions(session)` and passed INTO `combine_permissions`/`global_scope_permissions` as an argument — the decision core stays pure and the floor stays editable data. It is the ONE builtin whose permission set is mutable (`ensure_permissions_mutable` exempts it; `ensure_deletable` still refuses, since a missing baseline drops every non-admin to nothing) — `update_role` calls `authz.forget_baseline(session)` so the admin's own confirming read is not served from the request-scoped memo. **This narrowed live behaviour**: `page.write`, `cycle.manage` (+ its create/update/delete expansion) and `timesheet.view` used to be free for every active user via the second hardcoded set, which is how a member granted nothing anywhere could edit any wiki page and delete any cycle with no screen anywhere saying so; they are ordinary grants now. Deliberately a SECOND pass beside `effective_permissions` rather than a richer one: enforcement runs per request and stays a set union, the explanation runs when an admin opens one row. visibility helpers `readable`/`readable_keys`/`writable_check` (403 listing denied keys) are PURE over a `FieldAccessContext{role_ids, team_ids, has_manage}` (built from `authz.subjects_for` + the `require` union — see grant semantics below); OpenAPI properties carry `x-restricted: true` iff the field has any grant rows; **`default_value`** (nullable JSONB, spec 50 follow-up) — validated against the field type/options on create (`FieldDefinitionCreate`) and via `PATCH /fields/{id}` (`FieldDefinitionUpdate`, `model_fields_set` distinguishes clear-to-null from omit), reflected as JSON-Schema `default`; `validation.apply_defaults(definitions, values)` seeds it onto items whose create payload omits the key (called in `items.create_item` after the writable check — an explicit null is preserved, so admins' defaults never override an intentional blank) | workspace, events, auth, teams (RBAC + grant-subject validation). **Spec 87:** `DELETE /fields/{id}` (the `field.delete` atom had no endpoint) — definition + grants go (FieldPermission CASCADE); values already written into `work_items.custom_fields` are keyed JSONB and are left in place, unrendered once the schema cache drops the key. **Multi-project scope (spec 90 follow-up):** scope moved off the single `field_definitions.project_id` column onto a **`field_definition_projects`** association (field_id+project_id, both FK CASCADE) — **NO rows = global** (every project), rows = scoped to those projects; migration `8dc68ca67b05` backfills the old column then drops it. `definitions_for_project` (the ONE choke point every consumer resolves scope through — items/views/forms/screens/automations/workflow/SLQ) returns global fields ∪ those scoped to the project. `FieldDefinitionCreate.project_ids` (legacy `project_id` folded in as an alias); `FieldDefinitionUpdate.project_ids` (`model_fields_set`: omitted = unchanged, `[]` = promote to global, non-empty = re-scope) **widens/narrows** an existing field's scope; `FieldDefinitionRead.project_ids` + `FieldDefinition.project_ids` property (from `project_links` selectin). Router RBAC `_require_manage_on_scopes` = the atom on EVERY project the field touches (old ∪ new on an edit), or globally when global. **Spec 92 — grants moved to the generic `access` module:** `field_permissions` is GONE; read/write grants live in `access_grants` (resource_type=`field`), managed via the generic `/grants` API + `<AccessGrantsEditor>`, now **scopeable to projects and grantable to a USER** (not just role/team). The fields module registers the `field` ResourceSpec (read/write, write⇒read, default-open, `can_manage` = field.manage on the field's own scope) and resolves via `access.resolution`: `FieldAccessContext` now carries the actor's per-project subjects + the batch-loaded grants (`build_field_ctx`), `field_readable`/`writable`/`readable_keys`/`writable_check` keep their signatures (items seam unchanged) but delegate to `access.has_access`. `FieldDefinitionRead.permissions` → `restricted: bool`; x-restricted keys off `restricted_field_ids`; `delete_field` calls `access.clear_resource`. **Spec 96 — resolved writability for the SPA (disable un-writable fields up front, never edit-then-error):** `readonly_field_keys(session, project, *, user_id, role_ids, team_ids, has_manage)` returns the builtin field NAMES + custom field KEYS the actor may NOT WRITE in a project (custom via `field_writable`, builtin via `builtin_write_denied`, `PROJECT_MANAGE` bypasses) — per-(actor, project), item-INDEPENDENT (field grants are project/global-scoped); `GET /fields/writable?project_id=` (`FieldWritabilityRead`, any signed-in user) feeds the SPA's `useItemWritability` so editors DISABLE (dimmed, with a reason) instead of 403-ing on save. `item.update`/`comment.write` (already in `Project.permissions`) are the coarse gates; state transitions keep `/items/{id}/allowed-transitions`. Tests: `test_field_writability.py`. | `field.created`, `field.updated`, `field.deleted` | `openapi_augmentors` (injects live custom-field schema), `exception_handlers` (validation → 422), `on_startup` (warm schema cache) | **Spec 100: `extend_options(session, field_id, values, actor_id)`** — ADD options to a select, keeping every existing one, returning what was actually added. Deliberately NOT part of `update_field`, which holds options immutable: the dangerous edits are REMOVING or RENAMING an option, because items already store that value and would silently become invalid; adding one cannot invalidate anything, so it is safe to expose alone. Built for the importer (mapping into a curated select otherwise DROPS every value outside the list); **spec 107 cleanup exposes it over `POST /fields/{id}/options`** (`FieldOptionsExtend {values}`, field.update on the field's scope, dedupe = quiet no-op) and the Settings → Fields detail pane grew a real options section — count + filter (>12 options) + a CAPPED scrolling list instead of the unbounded pill wall, a TokenMultiSelect "Add options" control over the endpoint. **RADD-949 adds the other direction: `remove_option(field_id, value, replace_with=)`** + `POST /fields/{id}/options/remove` and `GET /fields/{id}/options/usage?value=` (the dry run, so the dialog asks with its number in it). "Removing is dangerous" was an argument for making the caller SAY what happens to the items, not for refusing forever — a field otherwise accumulates every value anyone ever typed and an importer's strays are permanent. The decision is forced by the field, never defaulted: **multi_select** drops the value from each list (a shorter list is still valid, no substitute exists to ask for); a **required single select** must name a SURVIVING option; an **optional** one may pass null to clear. Removing the last option, or naming the option being removed as the replacement, is a 422. The field's own `default_value` is migrated in the same transaction — a default pointing at a removed option would seed the dead value onto every item created afterwards, and it is the one nobody would think to check. Renaming is still refused, and for a sharper reason than before: every saved view, automation condition and form mapping naming the old string keeps compiling and silently matches nothing. **The rewrite crosses a module boundary the right way**: values live in `work_items.custom_fields` (JSONB), which is items', so `fields` calls the new public `items.service.migrate_custom_field_value` / `drop_from_multi_select` / `count_with_value` (`items/service/customvalues.py` — set-based statements, not a read-modify-write loop) through a DEFERRED reach declared as `weak_depends=("items",)`; a hard edge would be a cycle, since items depends on fields. Tests: `test_field_options.py` (11), which asserts each item's STORED value afterwards rather than the return count — a JSONB statement with `->` where `->>` was meant matches no rows and reports success.
| `access` | **Generic, scopeable, plugin-registerable access grants (spec 92) — the ONE ACL primitive every RBAC resource shares.** `access_grants` (resource_type + resource_id [string: a uuid or a stable key], subject_type user\|team\|role, subject_id, access [read\|write\|…, resource-declared], `project_id` NULL=global\|set=that project; unique per (resource,subject,access,scope); polymorphic subject_id/resource_id, no FK — a resource clears its grants on delete). **Plugin extension point** = `registry.register_resource(ResourceSpec)` — a module declares its `accesses`, `default_open` (fields open / views closed), `hierarchical` (flags vs ordered levels), `subjects`, `project_scoped`, `implied_by` (write⇒read), and a `can_manage(session, actor, resource_id, project_id)` authz hook; the generic `/grants` API + the reusable `<AccessGrantsEditor>` then work for it with NO new code. Pure resolution (`resolution.py`, unit-tested via test_field_grants): `SubjectContext{user_id, role_ids, team_ids, has_manage}` + `has_access(grants, ctx, access, project_id, spec)` (flag model — open until an in-scope grant restricts, then a matching/implied grant or manage) + `effective_level` (hierarchical) + `in_scope`/`subject_matches`. Service: `list_for_resource`/`grants_for_resources` (batch, no N+1)/`add_grant` (validates subject exists + access in spec + project + dedupe incl. NULL-scope)/`remove_grant`/`clear_resource`. `GET/POST /grants` + `DELETE /grants/{id}` (authz delegated per-grant to the resource's `can_manage`), `GET /grants/resources` (specs for the editor) — **which this row claimed for three specs before RADD-947 actually added it**: `ResourceSpecRead` was written for a route nobody implemented, so `AccessGrantsEditor` took `accesses`/`subjectKinds` as per-call-site props and rendered the project ScopePicker unconditionally, putting an "on <projects>" control on the page-restrict dialog whose write path answers "page grants can't be scoped". The editor now reads the registry (accesses, subjects, `project_scoped`), the props remain as an override, and an unregistered type keeps the old behaviour. Any signed-in caller may read it: it is the SHAPE of the access model, not anyone's grants. Adopters: **custom fields** (resource_type=`field`) — migration `6fc5f7c71481` moved `field_permissions` here + dropped it — and **builtin fields** (resource_type=`builtin_field`, resource_id = the builtin field name; migration `3d1e2f0f3823` moved `builtin_field_rules` here + dropped it + `rules.py`/`/field-rules`), both read/write, write⇒read, default-open, project-scopeable, subjects role/team/**user**. **Dashboard sharing** (resource_type=`dashboard`, migration `ef4be162afa1` moved `dashboard_shares` here + dropped it; identical spec to views but never project-scoped) and **view sharing** (resource_type=`view`, migration `925809931622` moved `view_shares` here + dropped it): accesses viewer/editor/owner, `hierarchical` (effective = highest held via `resolution.effective_level`), `default_open=False` (owner-private until shared), `project_scoped=False` (a view is single-project), subjects user/team — `owner_id` + `global_access` (the public level) stay on the View row; the user-merge/delete machinery repoints/purges USER-subject grants (`_repoint_user_access_grants`). SPA: the reusable `<AccessGrantsEditor>` (searchable **SubjectPicker** role/team/user + access + the **ScopePicker** multiselect dropdown [Global \| projects]) on every field's Access row + each builtin field. | projects, events, auth, teams, groups | `access.granted`, `access.revoked` | registry (`register_resource`) |
| `linktypes` | **User-definable, scopeable issue link types (spec 91).** The catalog `items` resolves link labels + symmetry through — no more hardcoded enum. `item_link_types` (key unique + immutable, name, outward_name, inward_name, `direction` directed\|symmetric, `system`, `auto_managed`) + **`item_link_type_projects`** scope association (NO rows = global, mirrors field scope); built-ins blocks/relates/duplicates/mentions seeded on startup (`ensure_builtins`) AND by migration `7c6a5417a89c` (idempotent) so keys already on `item_links` describe rows. `service.catalog`/`types_for_project(project_id)` (global ∪ project-scoped, auto-managed excluded from the manual picker)/`is_symmetric`/`label_for(def, incoming)` (inward when this item is the target). `GET /link-types[?project_id=]` (auth: any user — the issue link picker), `POST`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` instance-admin (system types: key+direction locked, undeletable; delete blocked while `usages`>0). `items` now: `ItemLink.link_type` is a validated KEY (not enum), `add_item_link` checks the catalog (unknown/auto-managed/out-of-scope → 409) + symmetry from the type, `ItemLinkRead.{link_type, label}` (directional), hydration + the DependenciesSection group by `label`. SPA: `Settings → Link types` (list + create/edit dialog w/ ScopePicker + direction). | projects, events, auth | `link_type.created`, `link_type.updated`, `link_type.deleted` | `on_startup` `ensure_builtins` |
| `cycles` | **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** **Home project (RADD-1291):** `cycles.project_id` (nullable, SET NULL; migration `d1291cyclehome`) is not a scope — a cycle still holds any project's issues; `GET /cycles?project_id=` answers cycles homed there OR holding its issues (`cycles.directory.for_project`), and re-homing needs write authority on both sides; series drafts inherit the latest cycle's home. Workspace-level iterations/sprints spanning projects (specs 14+60; **team visibility**: `cycle_teams` join — no rows = public, rows = those teams' members only, bypass = workspace ADMINS deliberately not cycle.manage [every member holds it]; `GET /cycles` filters via `visible_cycles`, get/stats 404 hidden cycles via `cycle_visible_to`; `team_ids` on Create/Read/Update [`[]` on update = public again]; cycle NAME still shows on item chips/SLQ — item-level data, documented; **nullable dates + DRAFT staging, spec 23**): `cycles` table (name, **nullable** start_date/end_date, goal). `CycleStatus` (**draft**/upcoming/active/completed) is **DERIVED, never stored** — `types.cycle_status(start, end, today)` is pure and returns `DRAFT` when either date is unset (a staging area — never active, off the roadmap/velocity/burnup until scheduled); `today` is injected at the service boundary (`date.today` in the router; tests pass a fixed date). `GET /cycles[?status=]`, `POST`/`GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /cycles/{id}` (writes: the instance `cycle.create/update/delete` atoms OR `project.manage` on the cycle's home project — `router._require_cycle_write`, RADD-1291; list/get on `item.read`). Validation: end_date ≥ start_date **only when both are set** (409); create with no dates = a draft, `CycleUpdate` uses the `model_fields_set` idiom so explicit `null` on a date clears it (scheduled → draft). Deleting a cycle nulls items' `cycle_id` via the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL` (and CASCADE-drops its stint history — complete cycles instead of deleting them if the carryover trail matters). **Cycle stint history (spec 56):** `item_cycle_records` (item_id FK CASCADE, cycle_id FK CASCADE, added_at, removed_at NULL=open) — one row per VISIT; `history.record_cycle_change` (re-exported via service) is called by the items service on every cycle assignment change (create + update — the complete-cycle carryover move routes through update), closing the old open stint and opening the new one; `past_cycles_by_item_ids` batch-feeds items hydration (`ItemRead.past_cycles`, closed stints only, deduped oldest-first). Migration `c536f26534fb` seeds open rows from current assignments; `scripts/backfill_cycle_history.py` mines historical stints from the event log's `changes` diffs (names → ids; stints of deleted cycles are unrecoverable). **Sprint close (Jira-style):** `completed_at` column = the explicit "Complete sprint" marker (`cycle_status` gained a 4th arg — completed_at wins over dates, so a sprint closes mid-window); `POST /cycles/{id}/complete {move_open_to: cycle|null, start_next}` (gated `cycle.update`) moves OPEN items (state category not done/canceled, not archived) to the target cycle or backlog via the items seam `items.move_open_cycle_items` (per-item `update_item` as the CALLER → authz/events/history apply; cycles→items is a **deferred import**, items imports cycles for hydration), optionally starts the target today (end = start + closed cycle's length, fallback 2 weeks), then tops up drafts. **Recurring series (per-label auto-provisioning):** `cycle_series` table (label, drafts_ahead, next_number, nullable start_weekday [0=Mon]+duration_days; unique GLOBAL `uq_cycle_series_label`) — a cycle belongs to a series by NAME (`types.parse_cycle_name`/`cycle_label`: label + trailing number, separators cosmetic, case-insensitive `same_label`), NO FK, so imported/pre-existing cycles join automatically. Created via `CycleCreate.recurring` (+`drafts_ahead`, optional `next_number` for a bare label — Jira-import continuity, numbering can start at 120); managed at `GET /cycle-series` (member) + `PATCH/DELETE /cycle-series/{id}` (cycle.update/delete; delete = stop recurring, cycles untouched). `ensure_series_drafts` tops the label up to `drafts_ahead` future (draft/upcoming) cycles named `label - next_number` (pure `series_draft_names` skips numbers an import already took); runs on cycle create + complete + series update, deliberately NOT on cycle delete (would make drafts undeletable). **Cadence (both-or-neither, 409 on half):** with start_weekday+duration_days set, provisioned cycles get real timelines — windows from pure `series_windows` (chained after the label's latest scheduled end, or today when stale/none; starts snapped to the weekday via `next_weekday_on_or_after`, so 14-day Monday cycles run back-to-back and unaligned durations gap to the next weekday), existing DATELESS drafts of the label are scheduled first (number order), and a recurring create without explicit dates self-schedules its first window. The complete-flow UI offers same-label future cycles as move targets. **Stats:** `GET /cycles/{id}/stats?assignee_id&team_id&project_id` (item.read) → per-state-category counts + estimate/logged/remaining totals (remaining = Σ max(est−logged,0) per item) formatted with the workspace's hours-per-day + **`points_total`/`points_done`** (spec 70 — always computed via the items seam `cycle_points_totals`, done = done-category states, same filters; UI shows points only where enabled) — composed in the router from the items seam `cycle_state_category_counts` + timelogging seam `timesheet.cycle_time_totals` (both deferred imports; both honor the cycle page's assignee/team filters). **`project_id` narrows the workspace-spanning cycle to one project's slice — project-scoped surfaces (planning page, project views' cycle handles) MUST pass it or a DEV handle shows TD time; the workspace-level cycle page stays unscoped by design.** Helpers other modules import: `get_cycle`, `cycles_by_ids`, `list_cycles`, `cycle_status` | projects, auth, events, settings (stats hpd), items + timelogging (deferred: complete/stats only) | `cycle.created`, `cycle.updated`, `cycle.deleted`, `cycle.completed` (payload: name, start_date, end_date); `cycle_series.created/.updated/.deleted` (payload: label, drafts_ahead, next_number) | — |
| `releases` | **Shipping is a workflow transition (RADD-1285):** `workflow_transitions.on_release` rows (one per from-state; they replaced spec 112's release-state settings) are what `releases.pipeline.sweep` performs, through `workflow.release_transitions`; an on-release row ALWAYS requires a release (`transitions.effective_rules`) — as an exact-from row it outranks an "Any → shipped" guard, so without it work could be hand-moved past the gate. Project-scoped releases/versions (spec 14) — the automation-writable replacement for the CI-writes-labels hack: `releases` table (project_id FK, name, version, status `ReleaseStatus` planned\|released default planned, released_at nullable, description, `Unique(project_id, version)`). Setting `status→released` stamps `released_at` server-side (and `→planned` clears it). Ordinary API resources a CI service-account/PAT or the automations engine can POST to and assign — no special path. `GET /releases?project_id=`, `POST`/`GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /releases/{id}` (create/edit/delete gated `project.manage`; list/get `item.read`). Helpers: `get_release`, `releases_by_ids`, `resolve_release(project_id, version)` (find-or-none), `list_releases` | projects, auth, events, settings, workflow (weak: automations, items) | `release.created`, `release.updated`, `release.deleted` (payload: version, name, status) | — **Spec 112 — the release pipeline (`releases/pipeline.py`):** the project's on-release transitions (RADD-1285, above) name the states; a project with none never opted in and every function here is a no-op. A merged PR moves work to the waiting state — which lives in the **`done` category**, so throughput counts the day the work was finished rather than the day a tag was cut, and the later move to the shipped state does not count it twice (the timeline collapses same-category transitions). **A version that BECOMES released sweeps, whichever path did it (RADD-1007):** every write goes through `pipeline.create_release`/`pipeline.update_release` (REST POST/PATCH, MCP `create_release`/`update_release`, the `release.publish` automation node calling `on_release_published` — RADD-1309 took it out of the connectors; **RADD-1310** `releases/automation.py` contributes that node, "Publish version and sweep": subject `project` (the release trigger's, or a named key), version = the event's unless the node names one (an override takes neither the event's name nor notes), permission `release.create`, the dry run says publish vs re-sweep and whether anything can move, outputs `release_id`/`moved`), which sweep every waiting item into the shipped state with `release_id` set on the planned→released transition — as SYSTEM for an automation, as the actor otherwise — which is what satisfies a project guarding entry to Done on a release being present. Re-saving an already-released version does not re-sweep; `POST /releases/{id}/sweep` (release.update), the MCP `sweep_release` tool and the Releases page's **Sweep** button re-run it on demand for work that reached the waiting state later, and are the recovery for a missed webhook. `on_release_published` find-or-creates the version (webhooks are at-least-once) and stores the notes WHOLE — the pipeline used to slice them at 2000 characters, which cut every substantial changelog mid-word (RADD-907). Deliberately "everything waiting", not commit-range-derived. MCP: `list_releases` (lean: no notes), `get_release` + `update_release` by (project_key, version) (RADD-908 — an agent can read and amend the notes of a release a connector published), `create_release`, `sweep_release`, `set_item_release`. Tests: `tests/test_release_pipeline.py` (12). |
| `items` | **Spec 86: `workspace_id` is GONE end-to-end — the workspace entity was eradicated (stage 3 dropped the tables); items are global-project-scoped only.** Work items: CRUD, per-project numbering (`TD-42`), `kind` hierarchy (epic ← issue ← subtask, max depth 3; **same WORKSPACE** since spec 80 — a parent may live in another project, cross-workspace → `409`, ladder violations → `409`), assignee (active user) + team (same workspace), state + priority + labels + custom fields inline; owns `item_labels`; **planning attributes (spec 14)**: `start_date`/`target_date` (Date null; `target ≥ start` when both set → 409), `cycle_id` (FK cycles `ON DELETE SET NULL`, same workspace → 409) + `release_id` (FK releases `ON DELETE SET NULL`, same project → 409) on create/update (nullable, `model_fields_set` idiom: omitted = unchanged, explicit null = clear); **`flagged`** (spec 24 — a first-class BOOLEAN attribute, NOT a label; NOT NULL default false, on create/update, omitted = unchanged); **`rank`** (spec 24 — DOUBLE manual sort key, **the default list order** when a query has no explicit `ORDER BY` — `list_items` sorts `rank ASC`, was `created DESC`; rank backfilled newest-first + new items get `min(rank)-item_rank_step` so the default appearance is unchanged but reorderable; reordered via `PATCH /items/{id}/rank {after_id?,before_id?}` = midpoint between neighbours with rebalance on float collapse; also `ORDER BY rank`); **`estimate_points`** (spec 70 — nullable NUMERIC(6,1) story points, 0–999/one-decimal [pydantic ge/le → 422], `model_fields_set` idiom on update [omitted = unchanged, null = clear], always serialized on `ItemRead` [null = unestimated — per-project UI gating via `SettingKey.ESTIMATION_POINTS` is the optionality contract]; SLQ builtin `points` [comparisons, IS EMPTY, ORDER BY]; seams: `estimate_points_by_ids` [reporting's points measure] + `cycle_points_totals` [cycle stats — (total, done) sums honoring the same assignee/team/project filters as `cycle_state_category_counts`]); owns **`item_stars`** (spec 24 — per-user `PUT/DELETE /items/{id}/star`, idempotent, `item.read`, no event; `ItemRead.starred` = the requesting user's star, hydration takes `actor_id`); owns `item_links` (source/target FK work_items CASCADE, `ItemLinkType` blocks\|relates\|duplicates\|**mentions**, `Unique(source, target, link_type)`) — `POST /items/{id}/links` `{target_id\|target_number, link_type}` (MANUAL_LINK_TYPES only — `mentions` → 409) + `DELETE /items/{id}/links/{link_id}` (RBAC `item.update`; rules → 409: no self-link, same WORKSPACE — cross-project links legal since spec 80, `target_number` still addresses the SOURCE item's project while `target_id` reaches across —, no exact dup, `relates` symmetric so the mirror is rejected; link add/remove re-hydrates + emits `item.updated`); **`mentions`** is auto-derived (spec 52 follow-up): `mentions.parse_issue_keys` pulls `#[KEY](KEY)` tokens from title+description and `service.sync_mention_links` reconciles the item's outgoing `mentions` edges on create/update (cross-project allowed, self-mention skipped, keys resolved via `find_item_by_key`, unknown keys dropped); excluded from the History diff (`changes._link_keys`) and from the DependenciesSection/roadmap/SLQ-`blocks` surfaces, surfaced instead read-only in the item view's Mentions section; reads embed `cycle` {id,name,status}\|null, `release` {id,version,status}\|null, and `links` {outgoing/incoming: [{id, link_type, item:{id,key,title}}]} — all batch-hydrated (no N+1, `hydrate` takes an injectable `today` for cycle status); **SLQ** text queries via `q` (spec 10, `items/slq/` — see "SLQ" below; ANDed with the structured params, its ORDER BY drives the sort, invalid → 422 `{detail, position}`; `past_cycle` field = closed cycle stints from `item_cycle_records` — carryovers, spec 56); **SLQ validate** `GET /items/slq/validate?q=[&project_id=]` (spec 55) — parse + compile only, ZERO work_items I/O; the editors' live per-keystroke validation, execution happens only on an explicit run; **SLQ autocomplete** `GET /items/slq/suggest?q=&cursor=&workspace_id=[&project_id=]` (spec 12, `items/slq/suggest*.py`) — reuses the lexer to classify the cursor position (`context` = field\|operator\|value\|keyword) and returns ranked suggestions with `replace_from` + quoting-aware `insert` (splices verbatim over `[replace_from, cursor)`); values come from live scope (state names, labels, users incl. `me`/`none`, teams, project keys, item keys, cycle names, release versions, registry select options, enum members); value context capped at 20, the bounded field/operator/keyword sets uncapped; scope = project (item.read) else workspace member; list filters (spec 08 — see "Saved views ↔ item filters" below): repeatable `state_id`/`category`/`kind`/`priority`, `assignee_id`/`team_id` (repeatable, literal `none` = unset), `label` (repeatable label name), `cf` (repeatable `key:value`, multi_select = containment; malformed/unknown → 422), plus `project_id`/`parent_id`; reads embed `parent`/`assignee`/`team` refs + `child_count`/`comment_count`, batch-hydrated (no N+1). **Transition guards (spec 61):** `update_item` captures `old_state_id` and — AFTER every patch field is applied (custom-fields merge included), BEFORE flush — a REAL state change calls `workflow.check_transition` (TransitionError → 422 `{detail, errors, from_state, to_state}` via workflow's handler; applies to every actor incl. automations/SYSTEM; item CREATE untouched); after the flush, a real state change also calls `workflow.state_moved(item_id, new_state_id)` (RADD-1383 — workflow tells the TRANSITION_CHECK providers, so approvals spends the unlock the move used; items imports no approvals code) — the cross-project move does the same. RBAC: create/update/read `require` the item's project scope; responses filter `custom_fields` to the actor's readable keys (grant-aware `FieldAccessContext`, subjects looked up only when a project field actually carries grants); writes run `writable_check` first; cross-project lists drop items from projects where the actor lacks `item.read`; `comment_count` counts only comments the actor may see (a per-project `internal_visible` set threads through hydration). **Reporter default (spec 62):** `create_item` resolves `reporter_id` by the `model_fields_set` idiom — omitted = the acting user raised it (unchanged), an EXPLICIT null keeps it NULL (email/public-form submits by outsiders; their identity lives in `mail_contacts` instead). The Jira importer omits the key for unmatched reporter emails so imports keep crediting the importer. **Bulk operations (spec 68, `items/bulk.py`):** `POST /items/bulk-update` `{item_ids ≤ bulk_max_items, patch}` (restricted patch: state/assignee/team/priority/type/cycle/release/flagged/archived + label DELTAS add_labels/remove_labels) — routes through `update_item`/`set_archived` per item inside a SAVEPOINT each, SKIP-AND-REPORT (`{updated, skipped:[{item_id,key,reason,detail}]}`, `BulkSkipReason` not_found/forbidden/invalid_target/transition_blocked/error) so RBAC/guards/events/automations apply per item and one bad item never fails the batch; `POST /items/bulk-move` `{item_ids, target_project_id}` (item.create on target, item.update per source, same-workspace) re-keys via the target counter, maps state/type by NAME (else same-category/default), clears releases, keeps cycles/labels, drops custom fields absent from the target scope, moves EXACTLY the selection (spec 80 revision — parent/child links survive the project change now that the hierarchy is workspace-scoped; children stay behind unless also selected, no auto-include/parent-clearing), writes **`item_key_aliases`** rows (old_key PK → item_id CASCADE) consulted by `find_item_by_key`/`get_item_by_key` on primary miss so `/issues/TD-42` keeps resolving after a move, and emits `item.updated` with a `changes` diff incl. project/key rows; `GET /items/ids` (same filter surface + visibility as the list, ids capped at `settings.bulk_max_items` + true `total`) = the SPA's "select all N matching" seam; **`GET /items/count`** (spec 75) — same filter surface + visibility, `{total}` only (both share the `visible_ids_query` builder in `items/service/scope.py`) — powers the dashboard slq_count widgets. with `project_id` also surfaces workspace-spanning views; ordered position→name), `POST` (item.read; `global_access`: view.create), `PATCH` (owner / editor grantee / legacy view.update), `DELETE` (owner / legacy view.delete). | `reporting` | **: every report endpoint takes an optional SLQ `q`** — the dashboard-wide filter seam: the service intersects each report's item-id universe with `items.bulk.visible_matching_ids` (uncapped, RBAC'd; new seam) BEFORE the history fold. Deliberate semantics: SLQ evaluates CURRENT item state, so `assignee = me` means "items assigned to me now — that subset's history". `POST /views/counts` likewise gained `extra_q` (compiled separately, predicate-level AND with each view's own query). Invalid `q` → the usual 422; a bad `extra_q` omits the count like a stale view query. **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Read-only analytics/dashboards (spec 16) computed on the fly from the event log + cycles — **NO tables, NO migration**. `timeline.py` reconstructs each item's state history from `item.created`/`item.updated` payloads: `build_item_timelines` walks an item's events in id order and diff `state.id`/`category` into ordered `(entered_at, exited_at, state_id, category)` segments (open final segment = still there); entering a `done`-category state from a non-done one is a *completion* tagged with the item's cycle at that moment, and the `(at, cycle_id)` trail feeds burnup's scope-over-time. Item scope is itself read from the outbox (`item_ids_for_project` via `item.created` payload `project_id`; `item_ids_for_cycle` via any event's `cycle.id`). Endpoints all gated on `item.read` for the scope: `GET /reports/throughput?project_id=&start=&end=&interval=day\|week` (items entering a done state per bucket, `[{bucket,count}]`), `/reports/cumulative-flow` (per-category counts at each bucket end, `[{bucket,counts}]`), `/reports/time-in-state?project_id=[&kind=]` (avg+median hours per category over *completed* stays, `[{category,avg_hours,median_hours,sample}]`), `/reports/velocity?workspace_id=&last=N[&measure=count\|points]` (items completed while assigned, per recent *completed* cycle, `[{cycle,completed}]`; `measure=points` — spec 70 — sums CURRENT `estimate_points` [null = 0] over the same item sets via the items seam `estimate_points_by_ids`, row shape unchanged, default `count` = exact prior behavior), `/reports/burnup?cycle_id=[&measure=]` (daily `{date,scope,completed}` over the cycle window, same measure switch; **409 for a draft/undated cycle — no window to plot, spec 23**), and **no SLA report**: the old `/reports/sla` (spec 63/65) is the slas plugin's own since **RADD-1386** (`GET /sla-report`, see `slas`), so this core module imports neither `slas` nor `csat` and declares no edge to either — the slas↔reporting soft cycle and the two undeclared model-only seams are gone. What a plugin's report folds with is PUBLIC in `service.py`: `bucket_start`/`bucket_starts` (ISO-week or day buckets), `matching_ids` (the reader's item universe — visibility always, `q` intersected) and `report_scope` (the `ReportScope` a cross-project figure carries). Windows default to the last 30 days; reversed window → 422. **UI (RADD-1386):** `reporting/ui` is a BUNDLED core package — the chart kit (`charts/`: `BarChart`, `StackedBarChart`, `LineChart`, `ChartLegend`, `ReportCard`, `CardBody`/`ScopeNote`, `ChartHeightContext`, moved out of `web/src/components/charts`; the host's report cards and the dashboard grid import it from `@radd-plugin-ui/reporting/charts`) and `report-contract.ts` (ids + prop types only): `REPORT_SECTION_SLOT` (`reporting.section`, `{projectId?, q?}` — the project and global reports pages render it after their own cards) plus `REPORT_CARD_SLOT` (card + scope note + loading/error/empty gate; the flag is `loading`, since `<Slot>` reserves `pending`), `STACKED_BAR_CHART_SLOT`, `BAR_CHART_SLOT` and `CHART_LEGEND_SLOT`, which its entry contributes so a plugin's report draws with the same kit, theme and dashboard plot height. **RADD-789 — the cross-project reports were unscoped.** `_matching_ids` returned None whenever `q` was absent, so the ONLY thing narrowing velocity/burnup/sla was the dashboard query — `sla_report(project_id=None)` folded every project's bookkeeping rows (and every project's csat ratings) into the averages, with the global `item.read` gate the sole thing in front of it. It now ALWAYS applies visibility and intersects the query on top, so the RBAC half cannot be skipped by omitting `q`; `csat/report.py`'s row gained `item_id` so ratings intersect the same set. The three cross-project responses are WRAPPED — `VelocityReport {rows, scope}`, `SlaReport {buckets, scope}`, `BurnupSeries` gains `scope` — carrying `ReportScope {covered: project keys, total}`: a filtered LIST is visibly shorter, a filtered AVERAGE is just a different number, so the figure states what it was computed over and the SPA's `ScopeNote` renders "across 2 of 5 projects you can read" — and nothing at all when covered == total, since a note on every report stops being read.
