| `notify` | Notifications + watchers (spec 26): an outbox consumer fanning item/comment/SLA/approval/participant/page events into per-user `notifications` rows, a per-event mailer, and an email digest. **Per-user policy is `notification_rules` (spec 118)** — one row per (user, scope, `scope_id`) carrying a SPARSE `channels` map `{kind: off\|inbox\|email\|both}` (migration `d118notifrules`; two PARTIAL unique indexes, because relationship rows carry `scope_id IS NULL` and Postgres counts NULLs as DISTINCT — one plain UNIQUE would let a person hold two `own` rules and leave the resolver answering with whichever it indexed last). `notification_prefs` keeps `email_digest` ALONE. **Six scopes** (`RuleScope`): relationships `own`\|`participating`\|`teams` (`scope_id` NULL — the matrix's three columns) and subscriptions `project`\|`space`\|`team`. Sparse is load-bearing: subscribing to a project must not overwrite what you said about your own work. **Resolution is `rules.py`, PURE + unit-tested** (`test_notify_rules.py`): most-specific-wins over own > participating > team-subscription > my-teams > project > space, taking the first rule with an OPINION about the kind, then falling back to the FIRST APPLICABLE scope's default. An UNSUBSCRIBED project is not a scope with an empty opinion — it is not in the order at all, or its `off` default would sit above `participating` and watching an issue would stop working everywhere nobody had subscribed. **Personal kinds** (`assigned`/`mentioned`/`participant_added`/`approval`/`automation`) resolve through `own` ALONE — the event chose the recipient, and an approver is frequently neither assignee nor watcher, so resolving `approval` by relationship would have handed them `off` and ended approvals in silence. The default matrix (`rules.default_matrix()`, built from each kind's `default_channel`) reproduces RADD-686's CHANNELS exactly: for anyone the old fan-out reached, zero rows = the old behaviour. `own` and `participating` are identical on purpose (RADD-686 had no relation, so any difference would be a behaviour change nobody asked for); `teams` starts OFF (defaulting it on subscribes every team member to their team's whole traffic on deploy day). A kind missing from the vocabulary degrades (`_default_for`) instead of raising inside the consumer's SAVEPOINT, and a test asserts `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` covers `set(NotificationType)` in BOTH directions. **Vocabulary is `kinds.py`** — one ordered `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` constant (wire value, label, description, personal), 13 kinds, SERVED by the preferences endpoint, because the SPA's own `Record<NotificationType,string>` meant a kind added on the server had no row in the UI and nothing failed. Three kinds are spec 118's and are `off` in every relationship scope: `created` (an issue FILED reached only its assignee), `updated` (a field moved that no semantic type covers, carrying the field names — a reprioritised/relabelled/re-estimated issue was silent to everyone watching it), `page_created` (notified nobody). **`GET/PUT /notifications/preferences`** serves `{kinds, scopes, defaults, rules, email_digest}` — `defaults` carries EVERY scope, because a subscription's unset cell needs one too — and the PUT is a FULL replace on `rules` (removing a subscription IS leaving its row out), bounded `max_length=200`, response read back off the stored rows. **Subscription targets are gated** (`targets.py`): a `scope_id` is a uuid the client picks, and the preferences READ turns it into a NAME, so an unchecked one is a lookup service for every project/space/team name on the instance. The gate is the seam each family's PICKER uses — `authz.visible_projects`, `team.read`, and for a space the `NOTIFICATION_SUBJECT` provider's `scope_names` (the wiki's `readable_spaces`; RADD-1385) — applied on BOTH sides (the write drops a target the actor may not name, the read refuses to label one, because a stored row outlives the access that created it); an unlabelled row renders `(unavailable)`. **Consumer** (cursor `notify.consumer`, dispatcher mirrors webhooks), `_HANDLED` = `item.created/.updated` + `comment.created` + the SLA timer events (`sla.breached` → `sla_breach`, spec-69 `sla.due_soon` → `sla_due_soon`, via `plan_sla_breached(type_=…)`, detail + `remaining_seconds`) + the spec-71 approval events (wire strings, approvals loads later: `approval.requested` → `APPROVAL` to each payload-listed eligible approver [approvers deliberately NOT auto-watched]; `approval.approved`/`.declined` → the requester) + the spec-72 **`item.participant_added`** (wire string too — participants loads later and is disableable; **RADD-978**: `plan_participant_added` addresses the payload's `user` ref ONLY, so a TEAM add plans nothing personal [team rows resolve live at fan-out and are ambient by design], actor-excluded, no `watch` entry because `add_participant` already auto-watched on the write path; the recipient passes `_allowed` through the Baseline's `item.read@participant`, i.e. through the very row being announced) + `page.created`/`page.updated` (spec 118, wire strings). **First start bootstraps watch-only over the whole backlog** (watcher graph without notification spam; needs `events.offset_exists`), and each event runs in a SAVEPOINT so a bad one is logged and skipped. Planning is PURE (`planner.py`, tested in `tests/test_notify.py`): actor never self-notified, one notification per user per event (assigned/mentioned beat state_changed/commented, which beat the generic `updated`); **reporter auto-watch (spec 62)**; `Plan.follow` is the ONE way a watch is planned and it refuses `types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` (RADD-996 — a wire-constant copy of `automations.types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` pinned equal in `test_notify.py`; mail intake creates items AS that actor, so auto-watching the creator had put `automation@radd.system` on every emailed-in ticket and fanned `commented` rows to a robot). **Fan-out is four sets, not one list:** `planner.Audience` splits the ambient audience into `own` (assignee/reporter), `participating` (watchers ∪ direct participants ∪ participant-team members ∪ mention targets), `in_my_teams` and `subscribers`, and STAMPS each recipient's relation on the `PlannedNotification` — the fan-out that built the sets is the only place that still knows which one someone came out of. `audience.recipient_ids(item_id)` (the spec-72 seam) is the `participating` member: watchers ∪ whatever every live **`NOTIFICATION_AUDIENCE`** source adds (RADD-1385 — `participants` answers with the CURRENT members of the item's PARTICIPANT teams), live at delivery time. `audience.item_audience` costs three queries beyond the old flat version: `service.subscriber_ids` (ONE indexed read over `ix_notification_rules_target`, matching project + team at once — a row's channels are deliberately NOT consulted in SQL, or precedence would have a second copy), the my-teams narrowing (from the RULES side first: a `teams` row has no `scope_id` to look up, and the other direction is a query per candidate for an answer almost always empty), and the team-member expansion behind it. **`own` is a deliberate WIDENING** — assignee and reporter are in the ambient audience whether or not they watch, so Unwatch no longer silences an assignee (it removes the participating relation only; the `own` column is the off switch) and on an instance built by a silent IMPORT, which writes no auto-watch rows, assignees/reporters start receiving ambient notifications and the `commented` mail that goes with them. Pinned in `test_notify_scoped_fanout.py`. **Every recipient still passes `_allowed`**: active + `item.read` on the project + the RADD-817 relation-scoped row check + the internal-comment filter (`comment.read_internal`, team narrowing via `teams.user_team_ids`) — participation and subscription never widen visibility. `_apply` resolves the CHANNEL BEFORE `_allowed`: same rows either way, but `_allowed` is a permission resolution + relation row check per recipient and a project subscription multiplies the candidate set, so an `off` verdict should cost a dict lookup. Roughly one permission resolution per SURVIVING recipient per event is the loop's scaling shape. **`service.create_notification` is the ONE write seam** (RADD-971): it refuses the system actor, resolves the verdict (or takes the caller's prefetched `RuleSet` — `rules_by_user` returns an entry for EVERY id asked about, because a partial dict read with `.get(id, …)` is easy to get backwards), returns `None` when the verdict is `off` (the mute moved out of the row's EXISTENCE and onto its columns, which is what makes email-with-no-inbox reachable), stamps **`notifications.inbox`/`.email`** (migration `d118notifchan`; `ix_notifications_user_read` gains `inbox`; backfill `inbox=true, email=false` — the inbox half is exact, the email half unrecoverable because the relation was never recorded), and emits `notification.created` for realtime. A caller that simply picked a recipient (automations' `notify_user`) passes no relation and gets own-scope semantics, which is what addressing someone directly means. Since RADD-972 that caller also writes `item_key`/`item_title` (via `items.item_ref`) when the rule fired on an item, so the automation line links `[KEY] title` like every other item-scoped type; an itemless rule keeps `lines.entry`'s linkless degradation. `item_watchers` (auto-watch creator/assignee/commenter; manual `PUT/DELETE /items/{id}/watch` emits `item.watched/.unwatched`, `GET /items/{id}/watchers`). `GET /notifications` (envelope with `unread_count`), `POST /notifications/read`/`read-all` — personal, no extra RBAC; all three filter `inbox IS TRUE`, and **both mark-read paths are scoped to inbox rows** because `mailer._pending` skips READ rows and an email-only row can never be opened, so an inbox sweep would otherwise silently cancel a send the person asked for. **Two email loops, one decision.** The per-event **`mailer.py`** (loop `notify-mailer`, `RADD_NOTIFY_MAIL_POLL_INTERVAL` 5s / `RADD_NOTIFY_MAIL_BATCH` 100, gated on `run_workers`) reads NOTIFICATION ROWS, not events (RADD-968 — the row already passed planner precedence, the verdict and `_allowed`; re-planning would be a second copy of the policy): `email IS TRUE` ∧ `emailed_at IS NULL` ∧ unread ∧ within `notify_email_max_age_hours` ∧ not backed off. `email IS TRUE` is a COLUMN because the decision was made at the write by code that still knew the relation — RADD-686's per-recipient Python filter could not be SQL while the only thing a row remembered was its type, which is also why the mailer could never honour a scoped preference. Comment rows render via `lines` + `mailrender.comment_reply` with the FULL body (`comments.comment_body` off `event_id` → the `comment.created` event's `entity_id`; **the event-type check is load-bearing** — every event carries an `entity_id` and for the item family it is the ITEM, so without it the transport got an item uuid as a comment id and the tick died on an FK violation, RADD-978); every other type via `mailrender.notice`. Delivery is the kernel **`MAIL_TRANSPORT`** socket (RADD-1385 — `mailintake` provides it; notify-owned `transport.NotificationMail`/`NotificationMailKind`/`MailFailureReport`; NO transport ⇒ email UNAVAILABLE, rows recorded undeliverable by `mailer.record_undeliverable`, inbox untouched), so a notification email threads on the item and a Reply lands back on the issue. `service.mailable_user` gates both loops: inactive ∨ address-less ∨ `UserSource.SERVICE` ∨ the system actor ⇒ skip AND STAMP, because the answer is a property of the ACCOUNT and a retry can only reach it more slowly (inbox rows for service accounts are left alone; the system actor is refused a row at all). **RADD-997 — per-row send backoff**: `notifications.email_attempts`/`email_next_try` (migration `d997mailretry`, no backfill — NULL means now) + `(email_next_try IS NULL OR email_next_try <= now)` in BOTH loops' selection (in SQL, so failing rows cannot fill the batch and starve healthy ones), `retry.py` holds the ladder (1m/5m/30m, then STAMP `emailed_at` — the terminal give-up has to be the stamp, or the digest picks up an address that has refused four times), `retry.record_failure` shared so the two loops cannot drift, and `mail.failed` emitted on the FIRST failure and the TERMINAL one only, via the notify-owned `transport.MailFailureReport` the transport is handed. The **`emailer.py`** digest (every `RADD_NOTIFY_EMAIL_INTERVAL`) is the slower half: `inbox IS TRUE` ∧ unstamped, grouped per user, skipping `digest_disabled_users`, sent through `mailer.send_plain` → the `MAIL_TRANSPORT` socket (RADD-983 — it used to dial `radd.smtp` off `settings.*` and sent nothing on an instance configured through Settings → Email). `lines.py` holds the shared vocabulary (headline/actor_name/entry + `MAIL_SUBJECT_TEMPLATE`/`MAIL_REASON_TEMPLATE`) so the two channels cannot describe the same type differently — they did, four sentences for nine types, until RADD-967. **Every user-addressed email says how to stop it (RADD-985):** both loops render with `mailer.preferences_url()` (`mailrender.preferences_url(app_base_url)` → `/settings/notifications`, the spec-118 tab — NOT the profile page the issue was filed against) and send with `mailrender.unsubscribe_headers` through the transport's new `headers=` parameter (`send_item_mail` merges them UNDER the thread's own Reply-To/In-Reply-To/References; `send_plain_mail` passes them as given). Contact-facing mail (ack, requester replies, CSAT) carries neither, by test in both directions. **Known issue (documented, not fixed):** an email-only row created while the mail transport is unconfigured (`transport.configured` false) for longer than `notify_email_max_age_hours` is never sent, never stamped and never digested (the digest filters `inbox IS TRUE`, so it cannot be the safety net for a row that was never in an inbox) — see `docs/specs/118-*.md`. **The wiki fans out here too.** `notify/subjects.py` is the non-item half of the consumer on the same three seams (it was `pageevents.py`, importing the wiki; RADD-1385 moved the wiki's answers behind the `NOTIFICATION_SUBJECT` socket); `pages/watchers.notify_watchers` is DELETED (RADD-719's synchronous in-request fan-out knew about page watchers only, applied NO read gate, and `page.created` was never wired to it). Gate = the subject provider's `reader_ids` (for a page: space role + every restriction on the ANCESTOR PATH, RADD-948 — the wiki's answer to give, not notify's to reimplement); internal subject comments gated by `comments.can_read_comment` (RADD-1385 — it was a hand-rolled space-scoped copy of that check). Page events carry `page` + `page_space` SUBJECTS (RADD-923, `pages/refs.py` + `entity_refs`), so the payload has the slugs a notification links with and the space id a subscription matches on — notify may import neither `pages` (load order) nor its models (the spine rule). **RADD-1056 — page comments notified NOBODY, ever**: `comments` has been polymorphic since RADD-717 and `_handle_comment_created` did not look at `entity_type`, so every wiki comment raised KeyError inside the SAVEPOINT and was skipped, @-mentions included; it survived because `page_updated` worked, so the wiki's notifications looked alive. **Files:** `types.py`, `models.py`, `kinds.py` (vocabulary), `rules.py` (pure resolver), `planner.py` (pure planning + `Audience`), `audience.py` (who could hear it + `Subject`/`own` derivation + `actor_name_of`), `mentions.py` (candidates → real active users, needed by the item AND page comment paths), `consumer.py`, `subjects.py` (non-item subjects on the socket), `service.py`, `prefs.py` + `targets.py` + `schemas.py` + `router.py` (the preferences payload, its gate, the API), `mailer.py`, `emailer.py`, `transport.py` (the `MAIL_TRANSPORT` contract, notify's side), `lines.py`, `retry.py`, `dispatcher.py`. **UI:** `/settings/notifications` (own tab under Account; the Profile section is a link) — `routes/settings/notifications.tsx` + `components/settings/notifications/` (`NotificationMatrix`, `ChannelCell`, `SubscriptionList`, pure `matrix.ts`), reading `queries/notifications.ts::notificationPrefsQuery`. The cell is a five-choice MENU, not two checkboxes: two boxes can say off/inbox/email/both but cannot say UNSET, and a control that renders inheritance as "unchecked" lies about the two cases a preference exists to distinguish. A subscription's unset cell shows the SUBSCRIPTION scope's default (`off`) — the "Mine" value would describe a notification that never arrives, since for an item you have no relation to the subscription is the only applicable scope — and a new subscription is seeded with exactly one kind: `created` for a project or team, `page_created` for a space (`created` is planned from ITEM events, which carry no space). **Tests** (counts drift — run the suite): `test_notify_rules.py`, `test_notify_scoped_fanout.py`, `test_notify.py` (incl. the migration backfill replayed through alembic's operations proxy inside the test transaction), `test_notify_mailer.py`, `test_page_comments.py`, `test_event_subjects.py`. Browser proof `web/scripts/notification-matrix-proof.mjs` (both themes) — it caught the matrix's columns wobbling 10px because a DISABLED cell renders one mark and a live one renders two. `docs/specs/118-*.md`. **History, superseded (the vocabulary still appears in old commits and issues):** RADD-686 stored a preference as two per-TYPE JSONB lists on `notification_prefs` — `muted_types` (inbox = NOT muted) and `email_types` (migration `d686emailtypes`) — with email REQUIRING inbox by construction, `service.set_prefs` normalising muted entries out of `email_types`, the mailer narrowing each candidate per RECIPIENT in Python via `email_types_by_user`, and a two-checkbox `NotificationPrefsPanel.tsx` on /settings/profile. Spec 118 dropped both columns; `set_prefs`, `muted_types_by_user`, `email_types_by_user`, `mailer._wanted` and the panel are gone with them. The personally-directed set is each kind's `default_channel` (`kinds.py`), with its **known asymmetry** intact (RADD-978: `participant_added` joined that set AFTER `d686emailtypes` had backfilled existing rows with the four it then held, and no migration corrects a stored preference by switching on a channel nobody asked for — `d118notifrules` carried those rows forward as they were). | events, projects, auth, items, comments, teams (no edge to any optional plugin since RADD-1385: participants, mailintake and pages serve sockets and depend on notify) | `notification.created` (what realtime pushes for live bells; payload carries `inbox` since spec 118), `item.watched`, `item.unwatched` | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (consumer + mailer + emailer loops); consumer-offset pattern |

WebSocket live updates (spec 27) — **NO tables**. `GET /api/v1/ws` (session-cookie auth on the handshake, invalid → app close `4401`); an in-process broadcaster tails the outbox from the stream head (**ephemeral — deliberately NOT a `consumer_offsets` consumer**; reconnecting clients refetch instead of replay) and pushes compact `{entity, event_type}` frames — no payload data, so worst-case misdelivery = a refetch of RBAC'd endpoints. Delivery predicate (`hub.should_deliver`, pure, tested in `test_realtime.py`; **spec 86**): `notification` events only to their recipient (`payload.user_id`); EVERY other frame to every AUTHENTICATED connection (the membership check is gone — frames are payload-free pings and the refetched endpoints are RBAC'd; `ClientInfo` is `user_id` only; frames carry no `workspace_id` (dropped with the entity)). Frontend `lib/realtime.ts`: one socket per shell (`useRealtime()` in `AppLayout`), server entity string → `Entity` tags (`SERVER_ENTITY_TAGS`), 200ms coalescing → one `invalidateEntities()` per burst, exponential-backoff reconnect that invalidates ALL tags once on re-connect. | events, auth | — | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (broadcaster task) **RADD-1237 (radd-hq/radd#9):** a query with no `meta.entities` is never SUBSCRIBED, so the hub never sends it anything — `itemTimelogQuery` was one, which is why a worklog logged over MCP sat unseen until a reload. migration `d841searchrel` backfills) so a relation-scoped actor (item.read@own/@team, RADD-823) filters FTS without joining `work_items` per query; item events write them (only when the payload SPEAKS about a relation — a partial payload can't null a good mirror), and `sync_relation_columns` re-mirrors drifted rows at consumer start + on `user.deleted`/`team.deleted` (user merge/delete repoints assignee/reporter in bulk SQL with no item events). **RADD-1030 — the index stopped hardcoding which relations it covers.** The fixed `own`/`assigned`/`team` mirror answers only the relations `items` itself registers; the RADD-841 mirror columns stay the fast path for own/assigned/team, the registry walk covers everything else. `tests/test_search_relations.py`. | events, projects, auth, teams, workflow, items, comments, access, fields | — | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (indexer loop); Blob API (`save_blob`/`read_blob`/`remove_blob`, default host, no routing) for jiraimport; `hosts.register_use_check` guards host deletion. Settings → Storage (instance admin). Garage is the blessed dev/test S3 server (`compose.dev.yaml --profile storage` + `deploy/garage/`). Originally: file attachments on work items (spec 29): `attachments` table (original filename, content_type, size, uuid `storage_name`) + a **storage seam (spec 33)** — `RADD_ATTACHMENT_STORAGE` selects **filesystem** (default, `RADD_ATTACHMENTS_DIR`) or **s3** (any S3-compatible store via the `minio` SDK: uploads buffered to the size cap then `put_object`; downloads 307-redirect to a short presigned GET with response-disposition; bucket auto-created on startup; `RADD_S3_*` settings). `POST /items/{id}/attachments` (multipart, `item.update`, streamed with `RADD_ATTACHMENT_MAX_BYTES` cap → 413), `GET /items/{id}/attachments` + `GET /attachments/{id}` (`item.read`; `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; **inline only for non-SVG `image/*`** — everything else downloads so hostile HTML/SVG never renders in-origin), `DELETE` (uploader or `project.manage`). Widths are BUCKETED (nine of them; an arbitrary width means a decode-and-encode per pixel dragged) and only ever DOWNSCALE — at or above the original, the original is served. A width request always PROXIES, whatever the host's `delivery_mode`: a presigned redirect would hand the browser the original bytes and silently ignore the width. Response met = first PUBLIC comment by neither reporter nor system actor (`comments.public_comment_times`); resolution met = first done-category entry. Engine = a periodic clock (`RADD_SLA_CHECK_INTERVAL`, NOT an outbox consumer — breaches happen when nothing changes) emitting `sla.breached` with entity_type **item** (lands in the History feed; notify fans it to assignee+watchers as `sla_breach`). Evaluation lives in `evaluation.py` (split from service.py); **`report.py` IS the SLA report (RADD-1386, moved from reporting):** `GET /sla-report?[project_id=][&weeks=1..26][&q=]` (default 12; `Actor`, so a public project's world reads it like every report; `item.read` on the project, else the member floor + `ReportScope`) — weekly buckets over `sla_item_states` by ITEM-CREATED week (met = met without a breach stamp, met-late = breached, wall-clock averages), folded with reporting's public `bucket_start`/`matching_ids`/`report_scope`, so the edge runs slas → reporting only. CSAT (`csat_avg`/`csat_count`, by the week the RESPONSE arrived) is a WEAK edge between two optional plugins: `weak_depends=("csat",)`, `CSAT_PLUGIN_ID in registries.plugins` checked per request and `csat.report` imported only after it, so a csat disabled at runtime drops the ratings (None/0) without a restart. **UI:** the `slas/ui` remote (`/plugins/slas/remoteEntry.js`, ui_api **1.16.0** since RADD-1396) contributes the report to `reporting.section` (project + global reports pages) and draws the dashboards' builtin `report_sla` widget through `dashboard.widget`, rendering through reporting's chart slots; the CSAT tile shows only while csat is loaded. **RADD-1394 — the timers are the remote's too:** the issue rail's SLA section (`SlaPanel`, `issue.panel.section` order 40, `GET /items/{id}/sla` under `["slas","item",id]`) and the **`slas.timer` list column / board-card cell** — an SDK item attribute (`itemAttribute`, sample = one healthy response timer for the card designer) whose data is the `slas.timers` query source (`POST /items/sla/batch`, re-read every 60 s, tagged `item`+`sla_policy` — the server entity type verbatim since RADD-1396, which the host's realtime passes through); the chips (`SlaChips.tsx`: nearest-to-breach pick, countdown/Paused/Breached/Met/Met late) moved with them onto the status-token tier (4.5:1 measured in both themes). Migration `d1394slacols` rewrote stored `sla` column ids and card-layout/preset cells to `slas.timer`. **RADD-1396 — the settings page and queue views are the plugin's too, and `web/src` names no SLA code at all** (`plugin-boundaries.test.mjs`; the one mirror left is notify's own `sla_breach`/`sla_due_soon` notification kinds): the project's **SLA settings page** (`ui/src/settings/`: policies list, the one create/edit `PolicyForm`, `MetOnField`, durations, the csat "After resolution" section through the SDK's `ScopedSettings`) is a **`project.settings.page`** contribution matched on `sla`, listed by the manifest's `NavItemSpec(section=NavSection.PROJECT_SETTINGS, path="sla", requires=("sla.update",))` — checked IN the project — so `/p/$key/settings/sla` is unchanged; and the spec-64 **triage queue is this plugin's view type `slas.queue`** (`types.SlaViewType`): a `ViewTypeSpec` with `list_surface=ViewListSpec(rows_path="/sla-queue-items", columns=QUEUE_COLUMNS, refresh_seconds=60)` and `sidebar_section="Queues"` — the host's LIST draws it over the plugin's urgency-ordered rows (`queue.py`, whole match set ordered before paging), no drag-to-rank, and the sidebar lists queue views in their own counted section. Migration **`d1396slaqueue`** rewrote stored `views.view_type = 'queue'` to `slas.queue` (downgrade restores it) and deleted the dead `sla` screen rows. With slas disabled a saved queue view shows `MissingPluginType` and fetches nothing. `queue.py`'s "no deadline" sentinel was timezone-AWARE beside the naive `due_at` stamps, so any queue holding a ticking timer and an untimed issue answered 500 (since RADD-1201); it is naive `NO_DUE` now, with a regression test in `test_grouped_queue.py`. Proof: `web/scripts/sla-settings-queue-proof.mjs`. CRUD `/sla-policies` (`GET ?project_id=` needs item.read on the project; `PolicyCreate.project_id` REQUIRED, workspace derived from it, updates can't move a policy; writes `Permission.SLA_CREATE/UPDATE/DELETE` — workspace-scoped atoms resolved via the policy's project's workspace, admins); `GET /items/{id}/sla` recomputes live (≤1 entry now); **`POST /items/sla/batch`** `{item_ids: [≤200]}` (authenticated; filtered to readable items via `authz.permissions_for_projects`) → `{item_id: [{policy_name, kind, due_at, met_at, breached, paused, remaining_seconds}]}` for list/board chips. Frontend (host, until RADD-1394/1396 moved the timers, the settings page and the queue into the remote — see UI above): rail SLA panel, **project settings → SLAs** (`/p/$key/settings/sla`, spec 67 — moved out of workspace settings; nav gated on workspace `sla.manage`; priority chips, business-hours HH:MM pair, up/down first-match reordering, info banner, no scope picker — the project comes from the URL), and an **sla card slot** (default OFF) on lists/boards/views/planning — one batch POST per surface page, re-polled every 60s, nothing fetched while the slot is off. **Due-soon warnings (spec 69):** nullable `warning_minutes` (int>0) on `sla_policies` — when a live timer's remaining active time drops to it (pure predicate `evaluation.due_soon`: not met/breached/paused), the engine emits **`sla.due_soon`** ONCE per item/policy/kind (payload mirrors `sla.breached` + `remaining_seconds`), fired-once via `warned_response_at`/`warned_resolution_at` stamps on `sla_item_states`; notify fans it to assignee+watchers as `sla_due_soon` (muteable), and the automations catalog lists it under Service desk so pre-breach escalation is an ordinary event rule. Policy editor gains a "Warn before breach (minutes)" field **RADD-1299 — targets say what "met" means:** each target picks a `SlaMetOn` (`response_met_on`/`resolution_met_on` + `*_state_ids`/`*_team_ids`, migration `d1299slamet`; defaults are the two rules that were hardcoded, so existing policies are unchanged): `first_reply` **RADD-1303 — SLA rights are project rights:** `sla.create/update/delete` are PROJECT-scoped under `project.manage` (they were global under `global.manage`, so only instance admins could manage a project's own policies); the router checks them against the policy's project, and the SPA gates the SLAs tab, the page and the sidebar settings link on `perms.project(project, slaUpdate)`. | `reply_by_teams` | `reply_by_assigned_team` (the issue's team; no team = anyone) | `done` | `enters_states` | `leaves_states` (first moment NOT in them — created elsewhere = met at creation; closing straight from Triage counts). Pure resolvers in `metrules.py`, facts gathered once per batch in `evaluation._met_resolver`; the reporter and the system actor never satisfy a reply mode. A third first-match filter, `reporter_team_ids`: `first_match` stays pure, callers pre-fetch `reporter_members` (effective membership, nested groups included). `validation.validate_rules` refuses a state mode with no/foreign states and a team mode with no/unknown teams (409). States and teams are stored by ID. Depends on `teams` now. | events, projects, auth, settings, workflow, items, comments, automations (system-actor constant), reporting (timeline + report helpers), teams; weak: csat (report ratings, RADD-1386) | `sla_policy.created/.updated/.deleted`, `sla.breached`, `sla.due_soon` (spec 69) | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (engine clock) |

`api_token` = a `read_api` token — an ADMIN's also exposes other users' emails, which is what makes worklog-author matching automatic on an LDAP-backed host; `webhook_secret`; active; verify_ssl; credentials never returned, empty-on-update keeps the stored value) + `gitlab_repos` (connection_id CASCADE, `full_name` = `path_with_namespace` UNIQUE per connection case-insensitively, `project_id` = the DEFAULT project for releases, never a linking filter, default_branch, last_backfill_at, `time_category_id` RADD-1258). `RADD_GITLAB_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (+ `_BASE_URL`, `_API_TOKEN`, `_REPO`) are **SEED-ONLY** (one row, once); `RADD_GITLAB_MERGE_TRANSITION_STATE` is GONE, and so (RADD-1309) is the hardcoded merge transition that replaced it: the receiver links, mirrors time and FIRES TRIGGERS — nothing else. `POST /integrations/gitlab`: GitLab does not sign bodies — it sends the hook's Secret token back verbatim as `X-Gitlab-Token`, compared constant-time (`service.verify_token`) against **the connection of the payload's project** (`resolve_for_payload` by `project.path_with_namespace`), falling back to every active connection only for an unrecorded project; an empty secret verifies nothing. `object_kind` decides the kind (the header is a fallback): `push` → branch + commit links, `merge_request` → an upserting link (open/merged/closed from `state`) — **every id spelled by `vcs/ids.py` since RADD-1254**: `commit:<ns/project>:<sha>` (spec 31 wrote the bare SHA), `branch:<ns/project>:<name>`, and an MR is `pr:<ns/project>:<iid>` like the other hosts' pull requests (the ref TYPE says which); `release` with action `create` fires `gitlab.release.published`; `tag_push`/`pipeline`/`deployment` are 200 no-ops until RADD-1255. **RADD-1309 — GitLab's own triggers (`GitlabTrigger`, registered by this plugin via `vcs.triggers.ConnectorTriggers`):** `gitlab.merge_request.opened|merged|closed` (by the delivery's `action` — `open`/`reopen`/`merge`/`close` — never its `state`, which every later edit of a merged MR repeats; `parsing.mr_action`), `gitlab.push`, `gitlab.release.published`; one event per LINKED ISSUE through `vcs.receiving`. The response is `{linked, triggered}`. **RADD-1259 — MR time mirrored into worklogs:** GitLab has native `/spend` time tracking and no timelog webhook; the MR delivery that added or removed time carries `changes.total_time_spent` (`parsing.time_spent_changed`), which triggers `timelogs.reconcile_merge_request`: GraphQL `project.mergeRequest.timelogs` (paged, `Timelog{id, timeSpent, spentAt, summary, user{id, username, publicEmail}}`), author email from `publicEmail` else REST `GET /users/:id` (admin token → `email`), negative rows (GitLab's own removal bookkeeping) dropped, `spentAt` DATE taken as sent (UTC — a dated `/spend 1h <YYYY-MM-DD>` sits at NOON UTC on that date, an undated one at the moment it was typed); GitLab REMOVES time by appending a NEGATIVE timelog, never by deleting rows, so `net_entries` folds negatives LIFO in creation (gid) order — the first live run dropped them and mirrored 2h35m onto an MR GitLab reported as 0, then `vcs.timemirror.reconcile` with scope `pr:<ns/project>:<iid>` and `ref_texts` [source branch, title, description]. Best-effort: a fetch failure is logged and reported in the response, the link half has already landed. **Backfill** (`backfill.py`, `POST /gitlab/repos/{id}/backfill`): branches, MRs (state=all — and every MR whose REST `time_stats.total_time_spent > 0` has its timelogs reconciled, the HISTORICAL import), default-branch commits bounded by `RADD_GITLAB_BACKFILL_MAX_COMMITS`; offset paging via `X-Next-Page`; project addressed as `/projects/<urlencoded path>`; idempotent by the two upsert seams. Admin CRUD `/gitlab/connections` (+ `/test` → `GET /version` with a token, a public one-row project listing without) + `/gitlab/repos` on the `vcsconn.*` atoms; Settings → Version control's GitLab tab (RADD-1262). | `sso` | **Single sign-on — a PROVIDER REGISTRY since spec 110** (was env-single-provider OIDC, spec 40). Tables: **`sso_providers`** (`kind` `google`\|`oidc`\|`github` — every kind runs the SAME code+PKCE engine; a kind supplies its ENDPOINTS and its PROFILE STRATEGY via `KIND_DEFAULTS` [`KindDefaults`: `discovery` + `profile`], Google's issuer pinned, GitHub (spec 121, RADD-1145) fully pinned with NO discovery and NO `id_token` — `idp.py` owns the wire: `metadata()` synthesizes the endpoint document for a pinned kind, `profile()` verifies an `id_token` for OIDC kinds or calls GitHub's `/user` + `/user/emails` (subject = numeric `id`, email = the primary+verified entry → `email_verified`) and returns the same claim-shaped dict either way, so `provision()` is kind-blind; the module-level `idp.transport` is the MockTransport seam; name = the login button's label, enabled, position, issuer, client_id/`client_secret` [stored as-is, replayed on token exchange — the webhook-secret precedent; reads expose `has_client_secret`], scopes, `auto_provision`, **`allowed_signup_domains` JSONB**, `require_verified_email`, `group_claim`, `admin_groups`, `source`) + **`user_identities`** (provider FK CASCADE, user FK CASCADE, `subject`, unique (provider, subject); joins auth's `_MERGE_REPOINT` so federated logins follow a merged person). Env `RADD_OIDC_*` (+ new `RADD_OIDC_SIGNUP_DOMAINS`) is **SEED-ONLY** — `seed_from_env` runs only on an empty table (the `ai/registry.py` rule); empty list + auto-provision seeds the `"*"` wildcard, preserving spec 40 behavior. **Identity resolution:** FIRST login matches an existing account by **verified** email (this is what lands a Google login on the person's AD account — it keeps its `source`, role and history), then pins to the IdP's immutable `sub`; EVERY later login matches on subject alone, so a mailbox rename can't fork the account and a recycled address can't inherit a leaver's. `require_verified_email` (default ON) is the hinge — linking by email is exactly what an unverified address would exploit. **Signup policy** gates CREATION only (an existing account signs in from any domain): `auto_provision` off ⇒ no; `"*"` ⇒ yes; else the email domain must be listed; **EMPTY list = no signups**. Domains normalized on write (`@Radd-HQ.com`/`a@HJarrar.com` → bare lowercase). **Role sync only when `admin_groups` is set** — spec 40 wrote `instance_role` unconditionally, so a Google login (no group claim) DEMOTED the AD admin it linked to on every sign-in. API: unauthenticated `GET /auth/sso/providers` (login buttons: id/name/kind only), `GET /auth/oidc/login?provider_id=` (no id ⇒ the only configured provider), `GET /auth/oidc/callback` (ONE callback for every provider — the flow cookie carries which; **refusals REDIRECT to `/login?sso_error=…`**, since this leg runs in an address bar where a 403 JSON body would be the page), `/sso/providers` CRUD + `/sso/kinds` + `/sso/providers/{id}/test` (issuer discovery probe; failures as data) — instance-admin. Per-provider discovery/JWKS caches, invalidated on write. The `sso` capability reads a process-local snapshot (a `CapabilitySpec` check is sync). SPA: **Settings → Sign-in** + per-provider login buttons (Google's four-colour G). `docs/specs/110-*.md`. **RADD-777 — a provider's DEFAULT ROLE:** `sso_providers.default_role_id` (FK roles, ON DELETE SET NULL — a tidied role list must never break the login path; migration `cc302ccad603`) is granted in `provision()` from the ONE branch that constructs a new `User`, and nowhere else: not on the `linked` branch (an existing account gaining another door) and not on a returning login. It is a `global_role_grants` ROW rather than a field, which is what makes "never undo an admin's later change" a property of the data — nothing reconciles grants, so revoking or rescoping it is simply never re-read. Re-applying it per login would be spec 40's demotion bug in a new costume, which is why `tests/test_sso_providers.py` asserts the revoke-then-sign-in-again case explicitly. **RADD-780/781 corrected its SHAPE**: one global role could only say "everyone gets Member everywhere", so `default_role_id` is replaced by **`sso_provider_default_grants`** (provider, role, `project_id` NULL = global — the same pair `global_role_grants` carries, because these rows ARE the template copied into it) and **`sso_provider_default_teams`** (migration `e780ssotmpl` backfills the old column into a global row before dropping it). Both write full-replacement (the views/sharing idiom). A stale template never breaks a sign-in: every failure logs and is skipped, which for ROLES turns out to be unreachable (the FK refuses an unknown role and CASCADE removes a deleted one) but for TEAMS is real — a team linked to an AD group after the template was written has directory-owned membership and answers 409. Settings → Sign-in renders the same grant list the Users and Teams panels use; directory teams are not offered. **RADD-782 put a RULE between them**: `sso_provisioning_rules` (provider, name, position, `domains` JSONB) owns the grant/team rows (`rule_id`, CASCADE; migration `f782ssorules` gives every existing template one unnamed catch-all rule, so upgrade is behaviour-free). One provider serves several populations — `@acme.example` and `@partner.example` arrive through the same Google button. **EMPTY domains = matches everyone, and EVERY matching rule applies** (not first-match): grants are additive rows, so a union is the only composition that cannot surprise, and it is what makes a catch-all useful instead of forcing every rule to restate the common part — the spec-102 storage chain picks ONE host because a file lands in one place, which is the opposite problem. Matching is exact on the lowercased domain (the `allowed_signup_domains` normalization), never a regex or a subdomain wildcard: both are ways to write a rule matching more than its author believed, and this decides what a stranger gets on arrival. | events, projects, auth | `sso.login`, `sso.identity_linked` | — |

Settings → Plugins links sso to Sign-in with no plugin named in the host: `settingsPathForPlugin` falls back to the page whose route segment a plugin's `settings.section` matches (which also gives Leave a Time logging link, and mailintake a Monitoring one). Boundary test: `plugin-boundaries.test.mjs` refuses `/sso/…`, `sso` and `sso_provider` literals in `web/src`. Proof: `web/scripts/signin-settings-page-proof.mjs` (real backend, 14 checks, incl. the page with sso withdrawn from the manifest client-side). |

Then exactly the spec-40 path: find-or-create SSO-only user (`password_hash NULL`), `RADD_LDAP_AUTO_PROVISION`, the instance role re-synced from the admin groups on each login, `radd_session` cookie. Base DN = `RADD_LDAP_BASE_DN` or derived from the domain. The login page shows the directory form only when the public LDAP capability is on (RADD-1224). Empty `RADD_LDAP_URL` = dormant (403). **Optional service-account enumeration (spec 49)**: `search_directory_users` binds with `RADD_LDAP_BIND_DN`/`_BIND_PASSWORD` and pages the directory (`RADD_LDAP_USER_SEARCH_BASE`/`_USER_FILTER`) to bulk-provision users via `scripts/import_ad_users.py` (created member, matched by email so direct-bind login links up + syncs role); interactive login itself never stores a bind account. **Spec 84 (AD depth):** team↔group links reconciled three ways — on LDAP login (the direct-bind connection also probes each linked team's DN transitively; `groupsync.sync_login_membership`), on demand (`POST /teams/{id}/directory-sync`, team.manage, 409 sans bind account), and by the **`ldap.groupsync` PeriodicLoop** (`RADD_LDAP_GROUP_SYNC_SECONDS`, default 3600; gated `run_workers` AND `bind_account_enabled`; registered via module `on_startup/on_shutdown`). `groupsync.reconcile_group` replaces a mirrored group's members from the directory — unknown directory members are NEVER auto-provisioned by a reconcile. Group ops live in `ldap/groups.py` (service-account bind): paged `(&(objectClass=group)(cn=*q*))` search (member_count = DIRECT `member` length, display only) + transitive member resolution `(&(objectCategory=person)(objectClass=user)(memberOf:1.2.840.113556.1.4.1941:=<dn>))`. Instance-admin endpoints (all 409 without a bind account): `GET /ldap/groups?q=`, `POST /ldap/groups/import {group_dns, provision_members}` (create-or-link team = CN, seat transitive members, optionally provision SSO-only `source=ldap` users), `GET /ldap/directory-users?q=` (spec-49 enumeration narrowed over cn/sAMAccountName/mail), `POST /ldap/directory-users/import {emails}`. `users.source` set `ldap` at provision; pre-84 `unknown` rows claimed on login. **Spec 85 (Directory settings + automatic user sync):** the module's FIRST table — `directory_sync_state` (kind PK `user_sync`\|`group_sync`, `last_run_at`, JSONB `last_result`; migration `12e3da9fab36`), written by both periodic loops + the on-demand pass and served by `GET /ldap/sync-status` (instance admin, readable without a bind account). ALL directory search bases now resolve through the settings cascade: the async wrappers (`groups.search_groups/search_group_members` take a session; `service.resolved_user_base/resolved_group_base`) resolve the instance-scope keys (`ldap_user_sync_base`/`ldap_group_search_base`, empty → `base_dn` at USE time) and pass the base as a plain param into the sync ldap3 fns (`search_directory_users(q, base)` keeps a None default for the session-less spec-49 script). Group listing uses the GROUP base; transitive member resolution uses the USER base (member entries are user objects). **`ldap-usersync` PeriodicLoop** (`RADD_LDAP_USER_SYNC_SECONDS` default 3600, `sleep_first`, start-gated `run_workers` AND `bind_account_enabled`; the `ldap_user_sync_enabled` cascade value re-resolved PER TICK): search the users base → provision unknown users (spec-84 import path: SSO-only `source=ldap`), update changed display names on ldap-source users (matching is BY EMAIL — the enumeration's dedupe key — so email changes read as leaver+joiner, not renames), and only when `ldap_user_sync_deactivate_missing` resolves true deactivate ldap-source actives missing from the directory via `auth.service.deactivate_user` (session-revoking; local/oidc never touched). `POST /ldap/sync/users` = the same pass on demand (instance admin, 409 sans bind account). Settings → Directory (`/settings/directory`) is the SPA surface (status card, user-sync card w/ Sync now, group table w/ import/link/unlink); the Users page keeps account admin only. **Spec 88 — import conflicts:** `ldap/userimport.py` — `plan_user_import` (PURE) classifies each selected directory user against the existing roster as `new`/`linked`/`conflict` with the reason for each match (`ImportMatchKind` email|username|name; Radd has no username column, so a sAMAccountName is compared against an email's LOCAL PART). `POST /ldap/directory-users/import/preview` is the dry run; the import endpoint now takes a per-entry `ImportResolution` — **overwrite** (one account adopts AD's email/name/source, `id` PRESERVED so its issues/comments/worklogs follow; 409 rather than stealing an email another account holds), **merge** (fold the look-alike into the AD-identified account via `auth.merge_users`, then apply AD's values), **create** (keep both), **skip**. Nothing auto-resolves — a name match is a heuristic and rewriting an email is not inferable. An entry with no resolution keeps the pre-88 create-or-link behavior. `adopt_directory_identity` leaves the password hash alone (no mid-migration lockout) but sets `source=ldap`, so the account becomes directory-governed (rename + deactivate-missing apply). **Spec 87 stale-group guard:** an empty transitive member search is ambiguous (genuinely empty vs. group renamed/deleted), and believing the second would revoke everything the team grants — `reconcile_group` confirms the DN still resolves before acting, raising `StaleDirectoryGroup` (409 on the on-demand endpoint, a warning + `directory_missing_since` flag in the periodic loop) and leaving the roster untouched; `sync_login_membership` holds removals while that flag is set, so the login path cannot drain the team one sign-in at a time. A broken link NEVER unlocks the team (user direction) — read-only stands while the link exists; unlinking is the deliberate way out, surfaced as an amber `AD group missing` badge + a one-click **Unlink and edit here** banner. That rests on `get_group` answering `None` ONLY for a genuine not-found and raising **`DirectoryUnreachable`** when the directory can't be asked at all (incl. the eager `auto_bind` in `service_connection`, previously outside the try) — an outage must never be reported as a missing group. | events, projects, auth, settings, teams | `ldap.login` | — | **Spec 100 — skipping disabled directory accounts.** `ldap_exclude_disabled` is a cascade-resolved instance SettingKey (Settings → Directory → User sync, alongside the sync toggles) that `base_user_filter` COMPOSES onto `ldap_user_filter` — `(!(userAccountControl:1.2.840.113556.1.4.803:=2))` — rather than baking it in, and skips itself when a deploy already hand-wrote the clause. It renders its declared sections through the SDK's `ScopedSettings`, a mirrored group's grants through `RoleGrants`, and owns its endpoint vocabulary and import types; the host keeps no route, constant or query for it, and the Server-status row and the Users page point at it through the manifest (`settingsPathForPlugin`). The legacy `/settings/groups` redirect is gone. Proof: `web/scripts/directory-settings-page-proof.mjs`.

The kernel half is the new `PageExtensionSpec` contribution + `registries.page_extensions` (declaration only — the server never renders a body, so there is no handler); `GET /pages/extensions` (each entry carries `source` — the plugin that contributed it, read from the kernel's `page_extension_sources`, so the editor's insert menu groups by contributor without the client guessing from names it may never have seen; RADD-748) drives the editor's insert menu, so a PLUGIN's extension appears in a running Radd with no edit here and unmounting removes it. Rendering is client-side: reading SEGMENTS the body at extension fences (`splitExtensionBlocks`) and renders each extension as React between prose runs; in the editor a fence is a real node with a schema-generated config form (RADD-745). The migration backfills by importing the parser. **RADD-943** — the second derived index: `item_page_links.derived` (migration `d943pagerefs`) splits the table into rows a person typed (manual, untouchable by any edit) and rows the BODY owns (`pages/mentions.py`, reconciled on every body change from the issue references the text carries, so deleting the mention deletes the link). `items/mentions.py` now parses BOTH reference forms — the editor's `#[TD-1](TD-1)` token and the URL form `](/issues/TD-1)` / `](https://host/issues/TD-1)`, link targets only — which is what `chips.ts` has rendered as an issue chip since RADD-711, so the derived `mentions` links between ITEMS inherit the URL form too. A manual link on something already mentioned PROMOTES the derived row rather than 409ing; unlinking a derived row is refused (the next save would recreate it). `PageLinkedItem.derived` reaches the SPA, which drops the unlink button there. `POST /pages/reindex` (page.manage) rebuilds BOTH indexes over every live page — the backfill for content that never passed the save path, and what finally makes `backlinks.reindex_all` reachable (it had no caller; the `POST /pages/reindex-links` this row used to name did not exist). `restore_version` reindexes both, a leg RADD-713 missed. **RADD-944** — the automatic "In this section" subpage index below every page body is DELETED; children are the tree's job and the author's, via `radd:children`/`radd:toc`. **RADD-945** — the Linked-issues section starts COLLAPSED always, not just when empty; the count chip is what makes that acceptable. **RADD-948 — a page restriction runs down the TREE.** `page_access` / `readable_page_ids` now require every grant set on the ancestor path (self included) to pass, so restricting a parent closes its whole subtree in the tree, in FTS and by direct URL. RADD-792 deliberately shipped the opposite ("guessing at it would mean someone finds their subtree quietly invisible") and thereby traded a subtree quietly INVISIBLE for one quietly VISIBLE — the worse failure, and the DEFAULT one, since sensitive material is naturally a parent page with children. Deliberately EVERY ancestor rather than the nearest: nearest-wins lets a grant on the child re-open what the parent closed, which is the same hole one level down and exactly what the space rule already forbids. `page.manage` in the space still passes. The batched path resolves ancestors that are NOT in its input (a search returns matches, never their lineage) from one `(id, parent_id)` query per involved space, and both walks are loop-safe — `would_create_cycle` guards writes, but a loop already in the data must read as a finite path rather than hang the request. **RADD-718** — new table `page_labels` (page, label; both CASCADE) — the ASSOCIATION lives here, the label rows stay the `labels` module's (new `depends_on`), so a page and an issue tagged `incident` share one vocabulary. `PUT /pages/{id}/labels` (full replacement), `GET /pages/by-label/{name}?space=`; labels hydrate onto both `PageRead` and `PageSummary` in ONE query per tree. **RADD-733** — PDF export is a top-level SPA route (`/pages/<space>/<page>/print?subpages=1`), outside the app layout, rendered by the browser's own engine; no server-side change. **RADD-745 — one cascade, not three:** the kernel gains `CascadeSpec` + `registries.cascades`, and ONE consumer (`events/cascade.py`) drains the stream for every module whose rows must die with a parent. `attachments`, `comments` and (next) `access` all key children to a POLYMORPHIC parent, which cannot carry a foreign key, so `ON DELETE CASCADE` is unavailable — each had answered it separately (two head-seeded consumers plus four remember-to-call sites). A cascade is a `DELETE … WHERE parent = ?` that usually matches nothing; it does not deserve its own cursor when 18 loops already poll the instance. The manifest field is a FACTORY, because the set is derived from a binding registry other modules populate at import time and must survive `registries.clear()`. **RADD-744:** both binding registries (`attachments.ParentBinding`, `comments.CommentParent`) now carry `deleted_event`, and each module's GC derives its parent map from the registry instead of a hardcoded dict — a plugin that registers a parent gets cleanup without an edit to a module it does not own. **Specs 102/103:** registers the `page` attachment parent binding at init (wiki pages own files; global doc atoms gate them; `depends_on` gained `attachments`) and exposes `pages_for_embedding` + `pages_by_ids` for the semantic embedder/deflection. **Spec 106 → RADD-828/893:** `public.deflect_public` — the tokened form deflect's seam — is GONE with the anonymous form surface (RADD-828 deleted the tokened `/public/forms` path; the RADD-893 dead-code sweep took the seam): authed deflection lives in `search/deflect.py`; the public KB box and `public_only` went with spec 74 (RADD-1147). **Spec 86: global scope — the workspace entity is gone; this module has no `workspace_id`, no singleton resolver.** Pages (spec 43; renamed from docs/wiki/KB by RADD-701) — the Confluence half. Tables `page_spaces` (unique slug), `pages` (space, self-FK parent tree, markdown body, float sibling position, `version`, archived_at), `page_versions` (prev content snapshotted on every content change; unique (page, version)), `item_page_links` (item↔page, PK pair). Spaces CRUD `/page-spaces` (read page.read, write **page.manage**; DELETE 409 unless empty or force). Pages: flat tree list per space (client builds the tree; archived subtrees pruned unless `include_archived`, manage-only), `POST/GET/PATCH/DELETE /pages` (page.write; **optimistic concurrency** — `expected_version` mismatch → 409 with current; content change bumps version + snapshots; moves cycle-guarded, don't burn versions; DELETE archives, `?hard=true` page.manage w/ live-child 409; `/unarchive` page.manage). Versions: list/fetch/`/restore` (= a NEW version). Links: `/pages/{id}/items` by item KEY (+ item.read; carries `derived` since RADD-943), reverse `GET /items/{id}/pages`. **FTS**: `GET /pages/search` over an expression GIN index (`to_tsvector(title||body)`) — queried live, no indexer (single-writer corpus). **Its declaration sits ABOVE `/pages/{page_id}`, and must (RADD-761):** Starlette matches in DECLARATION order, so a literal `/pages/<word>` written after the `{page_id}` route is answered by that route instead — `/pages/search` returned a 422 about parsing "search" as a UUID while appearing, correctly, in the OpenAPI schema and at `/docs`. `tests/test_route_shadowing.py` asserts this over the whole assembled app; reaching the routes at all means walking FastAPI's `_IncludedRouter` wrappers, whose own `path` is `None`. **Public pages:** spec 74's `page_spaces.public`, `/public/pages` router and `/kb` routes were deleted by RADD-1147 — a public space is the Public role granted to Anyone (the spec-121 §5 addendum below). Permissions: `page.read` (viewer floor) / `page.write` (member) / `page.manage` (admins), migration backfills builtin role rows (spec-36 pattern). Frontend: the pages plugin's bundled package (RADD-1392, addendum 6 below) — pages addressed by path (spec 124), the tree + page with History/restore and Linked issues, the issue-page Pages row, Cmd-K "Pages", `/settings/pages` (page.manage), realtime `page_space`/`page` tags. the SPA's space route now RESCUES that shape (a uuid no space owns is asked of `pageByKeyQuery` and replaced by the page's address) and an unknown uuid explains the two address forms and links to the spaces. **RADD-1244:** `version` moves WITH a history row, never without one — a live autosave inside the spec-122 window writes the body only (`PageHook.BODY_AUTOSAVED`); the collab room seals a session that ends without its final save (`seal_history`: row + bump on room drop / hub shutdown), so the header's number is always the row count plus one and a stale REST client still 409s. **RADD-1248:** `refs.page_ref` carries `path` (the readable address's page part, walked up the parent chain) and the `space` ref, so a consumer holding the ref alone can name, scope and link the page. **RADD-1249:** the archive browser is a list you can WORK — search over title and path (client-side, one space's worth), multi-select, and `POST /page-spaces/{id}/archived/restore` / `…/delete` over the selection, answering per page (`done` / `skipped` with the single-page reason): restore runs ancestors-first, delete deepest-first (a child selected beside its parent is removed by its own request, not by the parent's FK cascade a moment later), a page with live children is refused per page, and a page the actor may not touch is skipped, never a whole-request 403. |
| `collab` | **Collaborative editing (spec 122, RADD-1161) — one live Yjs document per wiki page, in this process.** `POST /collab/pages/{id}/join` `{role: editor|observer}` (`CurrentUser` only — the anonymous Actor never joins; NOT in the anonymous inventory) gates through the SAME `page_access.guard_page` the PATCH uses (editor ⇒ `page.write`, observer ⇒ `page.read`) and answers `{session, role, seed, page_version}`; `seed` is true for exactly ONE editor while the room's document is empty (held until that session's first update, its disconnect, or `collab_seed_grant_seconds`) — the client that seeds the document from the page's markdown, so two simultaneous first joiners cannot double the page. `WS /collab/pages/{id}?session=` authenticates the session COOKIE exactly as `realtime` does (accept-then-close `4401`; re-validated on the `realtime_session_refresh_seconds` deadline and on inbound frames, throttled to `collab_frame_auth_seconds`), **RADD-1181** also rechecks current page/ancestor/space permission on socket connect, every outbound frame, every inbound document update and the idle deadline; loss closes `4403` and requires a fresh join. It requires the session to be THIS user's for THIS page (else `4403` — rejoin), then speaks the y-websocket protocol through pycrdt-websocket's `YRoom` (binary frames: sync step1/step2/update + awareness). `channel.py` is the seam: an OBSERVER's sync step2/update frames are dropped before the room sees them, its awareness relayed; editors' updates are applied and broadcast. **Persistence**: `page_collab_docs(page_id PK→pages CASCADE, state BYTEA, page_version, updated_at)` (migration `d1161collab`) — the full encoded state, written debounced (`collab_persist_debounce_seconds`) and when the last client leaves, tagged with the `pages.version` the room tracks; a room opening with a row at the page's CURRENT version resumes it, any other row is discarded (something that was not the room wrote the page; markdown is truth). Empty rooms drop from memory after `collab_room_idle_seconds`; shutdown persists everything. **The write guard** rides `pages`' hooks (`guard.py`, the `automations/subscribers.py` shape): `page.body_writing` refuses a body write while the room has a connected editor and `collab_session` is not one of them — 409 `being edited live by Ada, Grace` (names via `auth.users_by_ids`) — and VOUCHES for a save that is (`live_editor`), which is what skips `expected_version`; `page.version_bumped` moves the room's tracked version after a room save, and after a body write by anything else (only possible with no editor connected) RESETS the room — clients closed `4409`, stored state discarded. `is_enabled` asks the kernel registry, so a hot-disabled plugin stops guarding. Rooms are per-PROCESS (docs/deploy.md). Tests: `test_collab.py` (6, real sessions + ASGI sockets speaking the protocol through pycrdt's encoders). **UI (RADD-1397): the client is this plugin's remote** (`collab/ui`, `/plugins/collab/remoteEntry.js`, UI API 1.17.0) — until then it lived in the host (`web/src/components/editor/collab/`, Milkdown's `@milkdown/plugin-collab` inside `RichEditor`, and the SDK bridges `useLiveSession`/`EditingNow`), so a disabled collab still shipped yjs and y-websocket to every browser. The remote contributes a `liveDocuments` source for entity type `page` (`session.tsx`): a page asks `useLiveDocument`, and the answer is an observer (reading) or editor (editing) session with the presence strip (`EditingNow`, faces from `auth.people`), the elected saver (`saver.ts`, lowest awareness client id, 1.5 s after a change — it calls the PAGE's own save with the session as voucher, so it knows no page endpoint) and the saving line beside Done (`data-collab-saver`); 4403/4409 rejoin (bounded), any other refusal hands the page back to Save. Editors get an `EditorBinding` (`binding.ts`) whose `bind` loads `bind-editor.ts`: y-prosemirror's sync/undo/cursor plugins built over the HOST's ProseMirror — `prosemirror-*` are shared singletons loaded with the editor (`web/scripts/shared-modules.mjs`), so keys and `instanceof` match — plus the seed rule. yjs/y-protocols/y-websocket stay the remote's (`room.ts`, loaded with a page's first session); cursor chrome is inline-styled on house tokens. Found on the way: Milkdown's listener publishes the doc of the last LOCAL transaction 200 ms later, which overwrote a fresher serialisation holding a colleague's paragraph that arrived meanwhile — the saver then wrote without it; a bound editor now publishes from its current document only. Proofs: `web/scripts/browser-live-editing.mjs` (mocked, in `test:browser`, with a synthetic y-websocket room `lib/mock-live-room.mjs`), `collab-plugin-proof.mjs` (live), `collab-proof.mjs`. | auth, pages | — | `on_startup`/`on_shutdown` (the hub); page hooks `page.body_writing` + `page.version_bumped`; SDK `liveDocuments` (`page`) |
| `mcp` | **RADD-739/740:** `link_items` / `unlink_items` — both ends by KEY (every other tool addresses items that way, and an agent that just created two items holds their keys and nothing else), with `type` enum-rewritten from the LIVE link-type catalog minus auto-managed ones, the same treatment spec 114 gives the project parameter; enforcement is inherited from `items_service.add_item_link` rather than re-derived. `initialize` now declares `capabilities.tools.listChanged: true`, and `GET /mcp` is the server->client SSE stream that makes it honest — it emits `notifications/tools/list_changed` when a POLLED FINGERPRINT of the caller's finished, already-filtered catalog changes. A digest rather than mutation events, because the surface moves for three unrelated reasons (a deploy, a plugin mounting, the caller's own scopes changing) and only the last is an event this process sees. Tunables: `mcp_catalog_poll_seconds`, `mcp_stream_keepalive_seconds`. **RADD-741:** `update_worklog` / `delete_worklog` — MCP could create and list time entries but never correct one, and derived time gets corrected routinely. Row-level authorization moved OUT of the worklog router into the public `timelogging.service.authorize_mutation` (+ `can_log_general`), which both REST and MCP now call, so there is one reading of who may touch a worklog rather than two. **Spec 86 stage 1: the `workspace` slug args are GONE from the tool schemas (search_items/list_projects/search_docs are instance-wide) and the `list_workspaces` TOOL is deleted.** Embedded MCP server (spec 45) — **NO tables**. `POST /api/v1/mcp`: hand-rolled Streamable-HTTP JSON-RPC 2.0 (initialize / initialized / ping / tools/list / tools/call; protocol `2025-06-18`; batch rejected; -32700/-32600/-32601/-32602 envelopes in pure `protocol.py`). Auth = the EXISTING PAT machinery (`Bearer radd_pat_…` → real User; session cookie also works) so **every tool call runs through the ordinary service/authz seams** — an agent can do exactly what its principal may. Tools: `search_items` (full SLQ), `get_item` (+comment tail), `create_item`/`update_item` (custom_fields **JSON-Schema generated live from the field registry**, same projection as OpenAPI), `comment_item`, `list_projects`, `list_workspaces`, + `get_page`/`search_docs` feature-detected off the docs module. A tool that survives has its project parameter rewritten to an **enum of exactly those project keys** — above `mcp_project_enum_max` (25) it degrades to a string naming the count, because a 300-entry enum costs more context than it saves. **Hiding is presentation, not enforcement**: every tool still calls `authz.require`, and a hidden tool invoked directly returns the same error it always did (pinned by test). The old show-unannotated fallback is REVERSED: a tool in neither REQUIREMENTS nor the registry is hidden and logged as a wiring bug. North-star: `milestones/mcptool.py` (`list_milestones`). **RADD-673 — names in, ids resolved server-side:** create/update take `type`/`parent` (key)/`estimate_points`/`cycle` (name), `log_work` takes `category` (name), `create_release` takes `status`, and `sweep_release(project_key, version)` runs the spec-112 pipeline step under `release.update` — the full CLAUDE.md tracking loop now runs over MCP with zero REST (pinned by `test_the_tracking_workflow_runs_entirely_over_mcp`). **RADD-672:** cross-project gates (`search_items`/`list_projects`/`list_worklogs`) use `authz.require_anywhere`, so a spec-113 project-scoped key is admitted where its scope holds instead of 403ing on the global atom. The similar rerank prompt ranks RELATEDNESS, keeping non-duplicate related issues (it originally detected duplicates only — models kept the literal twin and dropped the rest). Settings → AI (providers/roles/toggles/presets, Test button). Similar-issues is SEMANTIC-FIRST: vector neighbors ARE the pool when embeddings are up, before the optional LLM rerank; the OR-ed FTS pool runs only as the fallback when semantic is off/empty — one shared common token was enough to surface an unrelated issue as a top "text match", and its top-rank-normalized score always read 100%. NL→SLQ is dialect-aware (`items`\|`worklog` per spec 98 — the worklog prompt teaches `issue.*` delegation and validates through `compile_worklog_query`), repairs entity VALUES pre-compile (`nlrepair.py` + `fuzzy.py`: "jimmy" → the real account via the items autocomplete candidates seam; substitutions reported in the explanation — SLQ itself stays exact), carries live issue-type + work-category + WORKFLOW-STATE names in-prompt (small sets in the prompt, unbounded sets via repair) and a `Today is <date>` anchor. **RADD-1140:** the prompt teaches that fixed/closed/finished/resolved/complete/completed/shipped mean `category = done` and open/unresolved/active/pending mean `category != done` unless the user names a listed state (`types.STATE_WORDS`, one enum-backed table shared with the repair pass); in `nlrepair`, a `state`/`epic.state`/`parent.state` (+ `issue.` forms) value with NO fuzzy neighbour is rewritten to that category comparison (`RepairKind.CATEGORY`, operator flipped for the not-done words) or, outside the table, left as written and reported (`RepairKind.UNKNOWN_STATE` — the explanation says the state does not exist rather than the query returning nothing). `ai` now declares `workflow` (StateCategory). The `local` wire shape ships built-in CPU embeddings (`localembed.py`, fastembed, `radd[localembed]`; embeddings role only; `GET /ai/local-embed` catalog, `GET /ai/embeddings/coverage`). **Spec 86 stage 1: `POST /slq/nl` lost its `workspace_id` body field (router resolves the singleton) and the NL→SLQ prompt says "tracker", not "workspace".** Optional AI layer (spec 46) — **NO tables**, dormant unless `RADD_AI_PROVIDER` set (`openai` = any OpenAI-compatible base_url incl. LiteLLM/Ollama/vLLM, or `anthropic`). One provider seam `complete(system, user)` (pure payload/header builders both shapes; upstream failure → clean 502). `GET /ai/status`; `POST /items/{id}/ai/summarize` (item.read; description+comments+history digest, NOT stored); `GET /items/{id}/similar` — **FTS candidates ALWAYS** (new public seam `search.similar_to_text`, RBAC-scoped) + LLM rerank when enabled (`reranked` flag; lenient JSON extraction, FTS-order fallback); `POST /slq/nl` — NL→SLQ with the frozen grammar + live registry in-prompt, **compile-validated server-side** with one error-fed retry then 422 `{slq, error}`. **Spec 119 — `ai.validate`, the second contributed automation node** (`ai/automation_node_validate.py`, a SIBLING of `automation_node.py` rather than a mode on it). `ai.classify` ROUTES — enumerated answers, so it cannot invent a branch, and it says nothing in its own words. This one GRADES: the admin gives a quality bar in prose, the model grades each problem `blocking`/`minor`, and the node is a pure router (RADD-1329) leaving by `pass` / `fail` (any blocking) / `warn` (only minor) / `unavailable`, publishing its findings (`ctx.publish_findings`) for a Block or Warn verdict node downstream to relay. **An AI outage must not silently block intake**: a provider failure, a dormant feature, an unresolvable gate and a node with no prompt ALL take `unavailable`; what that does to the submission is whatever the admin wired. An unknown `field` degrades to a general finding against the live builtin + `cf.<key>` vocabulary rather than being dropped; `max_findings` is clamped into its schema's own range, and `or DEFAULT` is deliberately NOT used because a stored `0` is falsy and "report none" is not "report five". New `AiFeature.VALIDATION` + `SettingKey.AI_VALIDATION` + `config.ai_validation` + the `SettingSpec` — all four, which `test_ai_features.py` enforces. It also closed a pre-119 hole in `automations.service._validate_graph`: contributed-node schema + permission checks ran for ACTION nodes only, so every contributed GATE (`ai.classify` since spec 116) stored happily with its required params blank and took its fallback port forever. **Spec 120 — `ai.generate`, the third node** (`automation_node_generate.py`), and the one the other two make sense of by contrast: `ai.classify` ROUTES (enumerated answers ARE the ports), `ai.validate` WRITES PROSE at a person, this one FILLS IN NAMED VALUES the rest of the graph reads as `{{<node>.<field>}}`. Params: `prompt` (<=2000), the shared `include` toggles, and `fields` — up to 8 `{name, kind: text|enum, choices}`; ONE `complete_structured` call with a schema built from them, every enum field a JSON-Schema enum, so the model cannot answer outside the list (the classifier's safety property, per field instead of per node). An enum with no choices degrades to text; a field name that could never be a token is DROPPED, which keeps the write path's token check honest. `text` is always produced. **No `on_unavailable`** — unavailability IS the port: no values are published, so every token that would have read one misses and its action records a skip, and a graph that wants to react wires `unavailable`. A GATE kind because a contributed ACTION cannot name the port it leaves by (`_run_action` returns what it created and `_run_node` emits `out` unconditionally), and fixed SET arity because the packet's variable bag has one slot per node. `ai.classify` also publishes its chosen answer as an ENUM output. New `AiFeature.GENERATION` + `SettingKey.AI_GENERATION` + `config.ai_generation` + the `SettingSpec` — all four, per `test_ai_features.py`; the prompt's help copy carries the injection caveat (a generated comment posts unmarked, by decision). Review follow-up: `ai.generate` supplies `AutomationNodeSpec.check` — more than 8 fields, a field name that could never be a token, a duplicate, one called `text`, or more than `MAX_CHOICES` allowed values are refused on WRITE rather than truncated at run time (an unfinished, unnamed row is not an error); `fields_of` stays lenient on read. | auth, projects, items, fields, workflow, comments, search, settings, events, pages, timelogging | — | `exception_handlers` (disabled→404, upstream→502, invalid query→422); PROVIDES the kernel `SEMANTIC_CANDIDATES` socket (RADD-1384: `embeddings/candidates.SemanticCandidates` — items + pages, `enabled` = `semantic_enabled`), which is how search, deflection and Ask mode reach its vectors without importing it |
| `forgejo` | Forgejo/Gitea connector (specs 47, **111**). **Tables (spec 111):** `forgejo_connections` (name UNIQUE, base_url, api_token, webhook_secret, active, verify_ssl — credentials never returned, reads expose `has_token`/`has_secret`; an EMPTY credential on update KEEPS the stored one, the ai_providers convention) + `forgejo_repos` (connection_id CASCADE, full_name UNIQUE per connection, `project_id` FK SET NULL, default_branch, last_backfill_at). `RADD_FORGEJO_WEBHOOK_SECRET` + `RADD_FORGEJO_BASE_URL` are **SEED-ONLY** (one row, once, on startup — the spec-100/101 rule). **`project_id` is a DEFAULT, never a filter:** project keys are unique instance-wide, so a key in any repository resolves; the map exists for release scoping (spec 112), display and backfill targeting. `POST /integrations/forgejo`: raw-body **HMAC-SHA256** vs `X-Forgejo-Signature`/`X-Gitea-Signature`, verified against **the connection this payload came from** — `service.resolve_for_payload` looks the repository up (case-insensitively) and tries THAT connection's secret only; an unrecorded repository falls back to scanning active connections, so a hook registered before its repo row still works; inactive connections sign nothing. Pure `parsing.py`: push → branch+commit links, pull_request → upserting `pr:<repo>:<number>` link (open/merged/closed) — every id spelled by `vcs/ids.py` since RADD-1124 (a commit is `commit:<repo>:<sha>`, never a bare SHA, so the webhook, the backfill and the CI stamp share one row); same word-bounded key extraction as gitlab (deliberately duplicated — connectors stay independently disableable). Writes via `vcs.upsert_vcs_link` as SYSTEM actor; **RADD-1309:** no merge transition and no release sweep — `forgejo.pull_request.opened|merged|closed`, `forgejo.push`, `forgejo.ci.completed` (a terminal workflow run) and `forgejo.release.published` are triggers (`ForgejoTrigger`), fired through `vcs.receiving`; the response is `{linked, triggered}`. Admin CRUD `/forgejo/connections` + `/forgejo/repos` + `POST /forgejo/connections/{id}/test` (calls the host's `/api/v1/version`), gated on the **`vcsconn.create/update/delete`** atoms (umbrella `global.manage` — the dashboard precedent; declared here by spec 111, **moved to `vcs` by RADD-1258**). RADD-1258: `forgejo_repos.time_category_id` (FK work_categories SET NULL; NULL = the instance's Development) is the category a worklog mirrored from this repo's PRs carries; `delete_connection` calls `vcs.timemirror.forget_connection`. **RADD-1260 — PR tracked time mirrored into worklogs (`timelogs.py`):** Forgejo tracks time on PRs (a PR is an issue) and exposes `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/issues/{n}/times` → `{id, created, time, user_id, user_name}`; there is NO webhook for it and the PR payload carries no total, so the receiver reconciles on EVERY `pull_request` delivery (with a token; best-effort, reported under `worklogs` in the response) and the backfill reconciles each PR it walks (`BackfillReport.worklogs`). Entry date = `created` (Forgejo has no "spent on"), which the note says; author email via `GET /users/{username}` when visible, else the identity map. Everything after fetching is `vcs.timemirror.reconcile` with scope `pr:<repo>:<n>`. Tests: `test_forgejo_timelogs.py` (MockTransport; `timelogs._TRANSPORT_FOR_TESTS` hands the receiver one). Tests: `tests/test_forgejo_connections.py` (9), `tests/test_connectors.py`. | projects, auth, workflow, items, vcs, automations, releases | — (vcs/items emit) | — |
| `github` | **GitHub connector (RADD-1129)** — the Forgejo connector's shape for github.com and GitHub Enterprise Server: `github_connections` (name UNIQUE, web `base_url` with the API base DERIVED — api.github.com, or `<host>/api/v3`; api_token, webhook_secret, active, verify_ssl; credentials never returned, empty-on-update keeps the stored value) + `github_repos` (connection_id CASCADE, full_name UNIQUE per connection case-insensitively, `project_id` = the DEFAULT project for releases, never a linking filter). `POST /integrations/github` verifies `X-Hub-Signature-256` (`sha256=` prefix or bare hex) against the receiving repository's OWN connection, falling back to every active connection only for an unrecorded repository; `ping` answers 200; `push` links branches + commits, `pull_request` links PRs (`pr:<repo>:<n>`), `check_run`/`check_suite`/`workflow_run` stamp CI state, `release` with action `published` (not draft) fires a trigger. **RADD-1309 — it acts on nothing itself:** the merge transition and the release sweep are GONE; `github.pull_request.opened|merged|closed` (by `action`; a merge is `closed` + merged — `parsing.pr_action`), `github.push`, `github.ci.completed` (terminal states only) and `github.release.published` are triggers (`GithubTrigger`) that an automation builds on. The RADD project's own release loop is such an automation. **Every commit id is the canonical `commit:<owner/repo>:<sha>` in both the webhook and the backfill path** (the Forgejo split that duplicates rows is RADD-1124). Backfill walks branches, PRs (state=all) and default-branch commits (bounded by `RADD_GITHUB_BACKFILL_MAX_COMMITS`) through the REST API with `per_page` paging; releases are deliberately NOT replayed. Admin: `/github/connections` + `/github/repos` (+ `/test`, `/backfill`) on the `vcsconn.*` atoms (owned by `vcs` since RADD-1258); `github_repos.time_category_id` + `forget_connection` on delete, as forgejo. **RADD-1261 — the `/spend` comment convention (`spend.py` pure, `timelogs.py`):** GitHub has NO time tracking, so a PR comment carries the entry — `/spend 1h30`, `/spend 45m <YYYY-MM-DD>`, `/spend 2h <YYYY-MM-DD> note…`, `/spend 1h RADD-412 review` (key override), `/unspend` (forget this login's time on the PR); one comment may hold several lines. Delivered by `issue_comment` (only when `issue.pull_request` is set), `pull_request_review_comment` and `pull_request_review`; entry ids are `comment:<owner/repo>:<comment id>:<n>`, so `edited` UPDATES the same rows (deletion narrowed to that comment's ids via the seam's `id_prefix`) and `deleted` removes them; an unparseable duration is counted as `invalid`, never logged. **Authors:** GitHub hides emails, so the identity map is the answer — filled by hand, or AUTOMATICALLY from a push's `commits[].author.{email, username}` (`record_commit_authors`: each match records an email-matched row); with a token `GET /users/{login}` adds a public email. Backfill walks each PR's issue + review comments (`RADD_GITHUB_BACKFILL_MAX_COMMENTS` per PR, unreadable comments logged and skipped) and reconciles the whole scope. Tests: `test_github_spend.py`; Settings → GitHub shares `VcsHostSettings` with the Forgejo page. Env seed (once, when the table is empty): `RADD_GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (+ `_API_TOKEN`, `_BASE_URL`, `_REPO`). | projects, auth, workflow, items, vcs, automations, releases | — (vcs emits) | `on_startup` `service.seed_from_env`, capability `github` |
