| `scripts` | **Admin-authored Python, run from automations (RADD-1269).** A `core=False` bootstrap plugin (in `config.modules`, on by default, disableable). `interpreter.py` owns ONE uv-built venv per instance under `RADD_SCRIPTS_DIR` (`uv venv --python X`, then the Radd SDK client and every `script_packages` row via `uv pip install`; `uv pip show` reports what resolved). **RADD-1277 — it builds with no network:** `resolver_args` puts every existing WHEELHOUSE first (`RADD_SCRIPTS_FIND_LINKS` — the image's `/app/wheels`, holding the SDK wheel + its closure built by `pip wheel` at image build — then `<scripts_dir>/wheels` for an operator's own), then the admin's `index_url`, then `--offline` when the row says so; the SDK install is ALWAYS offline when the SDK is a bundled wheel (`sdk_is_bundled`), and `--seed` is gone (pip came from PyPI and nothing needs it). `script_interpreter.index_url`/`offline` are the admin's (migration `d1277scriptidx`; `PUT /scripts/interpreter`, event `script_interpreter.updated` with a diff, the URL's password masked everywhere — `masked_url`). `RADD_SCRIPTS_API_URL` is what `ctx.client` calls (the chart sets the in-cluster Service). `runner.py` runs ONE script: a fresh temp dir holding the bundled `harness.py` (the child-side program, never imported by the server) and the body as `radd_script.py`, the packet as JSON on stdin, a MINIMAL environment (no server secrets), the result on the last stdout line after a sentinel, killed at the timeout, stdout/stderr tail-capped. The child's `ctx` carries `event`, `items` (full read models loaded AS the automation's actor, capped by `scripts_max_items`), `vars`, `params`, `actor` and a lazy `ctx.client` (`radd_sdk.RaddClient`) bound to a SHORT-LIVED UNSCOPED key `auth.service_tokens.mint_ephemeral_token` mints for the actor and `run_body` discards after — a key can never exceed its account (spec 113), so a script cannot exceed the automation. `run_body` COMMITS around the run (the child's request arrives on another connection). Nodes: `script.run` (action; set|item arity; `outputs` param declares the dict keys published as tokens; a failure RAISES so the executor records it — `executor._run_contributed_action` now rewrites the plan entry `resolves=False` on an exception, for every contributed action) and `script.decide` (gate; `ports` param + `unavailable`; its plan IS the run, like `ai.classify`). **RADD-1272: the script's BODY lives on the node** (`params.body`, a fresh node arrives with the author contract as its default; `check` refuses a body without `main(ctx)`), so the graph is the script and the graph's versions (RADD-1268) are its history — the one-day script library (`scripts`, `script_versions`) is gone, and the unreleased `d1269scripts` migration was edited rather than followed by a drop. `script_packages` (spec validated to name+extras+specifiers — no URLs, paths or options), `script_interpreter` (one row). `POST /scripts/run` (the inspector's Test box: a body as typed, an optional seed item by key, as the caller — a dry run never applies an action, so this is how a script is tried), `/scripts/interpreter` + `/rebuild`, `/scripts/packages` (+ DELETE). Atom `script.manage` (global, implied by `global.manage`; mirrored in `auth.types.Permission`). Events `script.created/updated(changes, body hidden)/deleted`, `script_package.installed/removed`, `script_interpreter.rebuilt` — audited, never triggers. SPA: Settings → Scripts, the plugin's own remote since RADD-1352 (`scripts/ui`: interpreter card, packages, the author contract), under the SERVER nav group beside Automations (RADD-1272); `ScriptInspector` is the automation inspector for its nodes (the code editor, outputs/ports, timeout and the Test box); dynamic ports/outputs in `node-visuals`/`automation-outputs`. Tests `tests/test_scripts.py` (this process's interpreter stands in for the venv); proof `web/scripts/scripts-plugin-proof.mjs` (a real uv build, a real package, a real comment through the SDK). | auth, events, projects, items | `script.*`, `script_package.*`, `script_interpreter.rebuilt` | — |
| every module (RBAC) addendum | **RADD-890 — a module declares the permission atoms it enforces.** `auth/types.py::Permission` centrally enumerated all 88 atoms of every feature module (`item.*`, `page.*`, `worklog.*`, `sla.*`, `vcsconn.*`, …) while the kernel permissions registry built for exactly that (spec 93/A2, `PermissionSpec` + `CrudResourceSpec`) had ONE client — `milestones`. Two parallel systems: plugins contributed, core modules edited auth. Now every atom is declared on the manifest of the plugin that refuses on it, and the registry is what `all_permission_keys` / `permission_scope_of` / `permission_description_of` / `implied_map` / role + token-scope validation / `GET /permissions` compose from — no core-vs-plugin branch anywhere. **Who contributes what:** `items` item.read/create/update/delete · `comments` comment.write/read_internal/delete · `timelogging` worklog.write/delete + timesheet.view · `pages` page.read/write/manage/delete (SPACE-scoped) · `attachments` attachment.create/delete · `workflow` state.manage + the state triple · `fields` field.manage + the field triple · `forms` form.manage + the form triple · `webhooks` webhook.manage + the webhook triple · `automations` automation.manage + the automation triple · `releases` release.* · `views` view.* + cardpreset.* · `labels` label.* · `canned` canned.* · `cycles` cycle.* · `slas` sla.* · `teams` team.* · `itemtypes` issue_type.* · `dashboards` dashboard.create · `vcs` vcsconn.* (forgejo's until RADD-1258) · **`auth` keeps governance only**: `global.manage`, `project.create`, `project.manage`, and the user/role/member/service_account resources whose tables and routers live there (`auth/permissions.py`). **What auth still knows, and why:** the `Permission` enum survives as the TYPED ALIAS SURFACE (~1000 call sites pass `Permission.ITEM_READ`, and `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` — hence the seeded Admin builtin role — is computed when auth is IMPORTED, which is before any feature manifest exists; a registry-built enum would make the admin role a function of import order), together with one scope token per atom (`_PROJECT_SCOPED`/`_SPACE_SCOPED`). Both copies are ratcheted by `tests/test_permission_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, scopes agree, one owner per atom, and auth's manifest == the governance set. **Implication edges moved with the atoms**: a `CrudResourceSpec.manage` umbrella, `PermissionSpec.implied_by` (declared in spec 93, read by NOTHING until now — a plugin atom could not ride an umbrella at all), and a new `PermissionSpec.implies` for the one thing `implied_by` cannot express — a relation-qualified form that is not itself a catalog atom (`item.update` → `attachment.delete@own`, RADD-790/816). `auth.types.IMPLIED_PERMISSIONS`, `CRUD_RESOURCES`, `CRUD_MATRIX` and `ResourceSpec` are GONE; descriptions are read from the registry directly (RADD-1404 removed the `PERMISSION_DESCRIPTIONS` mapping that only a test read). Behaviour-identical: the 92-row permission catalog (scope, prose, resource, action, order) hashes the same before and after. Two deliberate consequences of an atom having an owner at last — a token scope may now name a plugin atom (`scopes._atoms` validates against the live catalog, not the enum, so the spec-113 intersection no longer silently drops one), and uninstalling a `core=False` module sweeps ITS atoms from stored roles (RADD-818, which previously had nothing to sweep). |
| every module (settings) addendum | **RADD-891 — a module declares the scalar settings it reads.** `settings/types.py::SettingKey` centrally enumerated every feature module's tunables (`ai_*` ×7, `ldap_*` ×11, `csat_enabled`, `release_*` ×2, the four timesheet/timelog keys, `workflow_transition_mode`, `estimation_points` — 27 in all) in one hardcoded dict, while the kernel fields built for exactly that (spec 93, `RaddPlugin.settings_keys`) were read by NOTHING — the dead-code audit's SettingKey finding (`research/audit-2026-08/01-architecture-kernel-plugin.md` §5). Mirrors RADD-890's fix for `auth.types.Permission`, one registry over: every key is now declared via `settings_keys=(SettingSpec(...),),)` on the manifest of the module that RESOLVES it (`settings.service.resolve`/`.set_value`/`.list_for_scope`), and `settings` validates against the kernel registry instead of hardcoding every feature's keys. **Who contributes what:** `timelogging` work_week_days + timelog_hours_per_day + timesheet_day_min/max_hours (the timesheet's four tunables — `work_week_days` is also read by `slas`/`projects`, but timelogging is where the timesheet grid and its outlier flags live) · `workflow` workflow_transition_mode · `csat` csat_enabled · `items` estimation_points (resolved directly by the SPA via `GET /scoped-settings/resolve` — `usePointsEnabled` — not by server code, but items is still the true owner: it gates the `estimate_points` column) · `ldap` the eleven `ldap_*` connection/sync keys · `ai` its `ai_*` feature toggles · **`settings` declares none of its own** — it is pure cascade mechanism (asserted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`). **What `settings` still knows, and why:** `SettingKey` survives as the TYPED ALIAS SURFACE — it types a FastAPI query param (`GET/DELETE /scoped-settings`) and a pydantic field (`ScopedSettingWrite.key`), both fixed at `settings`'s own import time, which happens at a FIXED, early position in `config.Settings.modules` (right after `auth`) — long before `ai`/`ldap`/`releases`/`timelogging`/`workflow`/`items`/`csat` have registered theirs. A registry-built `SettingKey` would make its membership a function of `RADD_MODULES` order. Ratcheted by `tests/test_setting_ownership.py`: registry ⊇ enum, registry ⊆ enum, one owner per key, every key's label+description present, type/scopes are known values, and every `SettingSpec.default` resolves without raising. **Unlike `Permission`**, nothing in `settings` computes eagerly from the full key set at ITS OWN import time — there is no settings equivalent of auth's `PROJECT_PERMISSIONS` — so there is no static per-key scope/type table to keep in sync; `setting_spec(key)` reads type/scopes/prose/default LIVE off `radd.kernel.registries.settings` on every call. `SettingSpec` itself moved OUT to the kernel (`kernel/specs.py`) — plain `str` `type`/`scopes` rather than `settings.types`'s enums (kernel purity forbids importing a module's enum), with `.default` as a kernel property doing the `getattr(config.Settings, config_attr or key)` dynamic dispatch (kernel's own domain per its charter: "config/loader/lifecycle… the settings platform"). Behaviour-identical: booting `create_app()` and diffing the resolved catalog (every key's type/scopes/label/description/choices/secret/config_attr/default) before and after RADD-891 hashes the same. |
| every module (fact providers) addendum | **RADD-892 — the aggregation inversion: a module declares the facts about ITSELF, and the consumer iterates.** Three registries with one shape, closing the audit's `research/audit-2026-08/01-architecture-kernel-plugin.md` §1/§2/§4 findings. **`nav_facts` (`NavFactSpec{key, resolve(session,user)->bool}`)** — auth's `_nav_facts` imported `timelogging.service` and `forms.portal` behind try/except ImportError to answer RADD-843's two area-visibility booleans, which inverted the load order (both modules load AFTER auth); now `timelogging` contributes `timesheet` (`service.nav_timesheet_visible`) and `forms` contributes `portal` (`portal.nav_portal_visible`, new, beside `list_portal_forms` so the nav answer and the page cannot disagree), and `MeRead.nav` is a `dict[str, bool]` built from the registry at REQUEST time (an unloaded module leaves its key ABSENT, which the SPA's `known(v) => v !== false` already reads as visible — same fail-open, no `NavFacts` schema class). **`grant_scopes` (`GrantScopeSpec{key, labels, exists, reach?}`)** — what a role grant may be BOUND to: `projects` contributes `project` (`service.project_keys` + new `service.project_exists`), `pages` contributes `space` (`pages/grantscope.py`: names, existence, and `reach` → `(readable_spaces, total_spaces)`). This killed the LAST entry in `tests/test_module_contracts.py::MODEL_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST` — auth reached into `pages.models.PageSpace` from `authz_explain._space_names` (now the generic `authz_explain.scope_labels(session, kind, ids)`), from `router.user_resource_access`'s space counts, and from `grants.create_grant`'s scope check (now `_check_scope`, which validates whichever scope column is set against the registry). `reach` is OPTIONAL and `project` does not supply it: how many projects someone can read is an atom question auth answers with `require_anywhere`, while space readability is a per-space ACL only pages can compute. **The set of scopes stays CLOSED** — `key` names the `<key>_id` column on `global_role_grants`, so a third kind is still a migration in auth; the registry inverts the KNOWLEDGE, not the schema. **`project_purges` (`ProjectPurgeSpec{name, tables, order}`)** — see the jiraimport row. **What did NOT invert, deliberately:** teams and groups as grant SUBJECTS stay direct `weak_depends` service calls (`teams.user_team_ids`, `groups.user_group_ids`, `*_by_ids`), because `global_role_grants` carries a `team_id` and a `group_id` COLUMN — the subject set is closed by auth's own schema, so a subject registry would advertise an extensibility no column list can honour. auth's `weak_depends` is now `(access, groups, teams)`, down from six. |
| every module (audit diffs) addendum | **Spec 123 — every `*.updated` emitter says WHAT changed.** `kernel/changes.py` (`snapshot`/`column_fields`/`diff`/`diff_object`/`collection_change`/`hidden_change`; exported as `radd.sdk.changes`) is the one shape — `{field, from, to}`, `{field, added, removed}`, `{field}` for a hidden value — resolved to NAMES at write time (owner → user name, grant → subject + scope, field scope → project keys, worklog → category name, page parent → title). Secrets (client secrets, API keys, S3 credentials, PATs, webhook secrets, mail passwords) and bodies (comment, page, canned response, automation graph, form fields, card layout) are `hidden`. `tests/test_event_changes.py` holds two EMPTY burn-down contracts: every registered `*.updated`/`*.changed` type declares `has_changes=True`, and every event type a module's `*Event` enum can emit is registered (48 were not when the contract landed). New audited writes: `setting.changed`, `project.public_access_changed`, `page_space.public_access_changed`, `sso_provider.*`, `ai_provider.*`/`ai_role.changed`/`ai_preset.*`, `storage_host.*`/`storage_rule.*`, `forgejo_`/`github_`/`jira_`/`confluence_connection.*` (+ repos), `mail_source.*`/`mail_sender.*`/`mail_rule.*`, `work_category.*`, `project.timelogging_changed`, `state_category.updated`, `plugin.contributions_changed`, a comment's `resolved`. None is an automation trigger (`trigger=False`); the trigger snapshot stays at 75. | — | — | — |
| `kernel` (entity host) addendum | **RADD-892 — `kernel/entities.py` imports no module, at load OR at request.** The generated CRUD handlers reached auth/projects/events through deferred imports (`from radd.modules.auth import authz` inside the handler), which made the kernel depend on three plugins it sits underneath. `kernel/hosts.py` defines **`EntityHost`** — four operations, because those are the four decisions the kernel must not make: `current_user(request, session)`, `require(session, user, atom, project_id=)`, `visible_rows(session, user, entity_key, rows)` (the item.read-per-project pass + the RADD-817 relation narrowing) and `emit(...)`. `auth/entityhost.py` implements it and installs it at auth's import (`hosts.set_entity_host`); the slot is module state rather than a `KernelRegistries` field precisely because `registries.clear()` would wipe it with no import left to replay it. Resolution is per REQUEST: the entity routers are BUILT while plugins are still loading, so the current-user dependency is a kernel-owned `_acting_user(request, session)` that defers to the host — a build-time `Depends(CurrentUser)` would have re-imported the very module this removes. `register_entity` also auto-registers a `ProjectPurgeSpec` for a project-scoped entity, which is what makes the north-star promise hold in both directions: declare an entity, write no model code AND no teardown code. |
| `auth`/`items`/`comments` addendum | **RADD-1148 — a per-account write throttle (spec 121 §9).** `auth/throttle.py` generalised: one `_SlidingWindow` (bounded keys + bounded history) now sits under both `LoginThrottle` and the new `WriteThrottle`, keyed `(WriteBucket, user id)` — `item.create` / `comment.create`. `check_write(user, bucket)` is the seam the routers consult AFTER authentication and BEFORE the service call (`POST /items`, `POST /items/{id}/comments`, `POST /{entity_type}/{id}/comments`); it exempts instance admins and the automation actor (a literal uuid, the notify idiom, pinned to `automations.types.SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID` by test). Refusal is 429 + `Retry-After`. Tunables `RADD_WRITE_WINDOW_SECONDS` / `RADD_ITEM_CREATES_PER_WINDOW` / `RADD_COMMENTS_PER_WINDOW` / `RADD_WRITE_THROTTLE_BUCKET_LIMIT` (`docs/deploy.md`). Service-level creation (mail intake, forms, automations, MCP) is deliberately NOT throttled — the window guards the open HTTP routes, and per-process counters carry the login throttle's single-replica caveat. |
| `views`/`web` addendum (RADD-1394) | **Plugins contribute list columns and board-card cells.** SDK 1.15 `itemAttribute({id, label, width, minWidth, source, sample?, render})` builds a contribution to the new `item.attribute` slot (keyed by `match` = the id; the column's data rides the new generic `SlotContribution.meta`); `source` names one of the plugin's own `querySources`, called with `{ids}` (≤ `ITEM_ATTRIBUTE_BATCH_MAX` = 200) → `{[itemId]: value}`. **Namespace:** an id must be `<plugin>.<name>` of its registering plugin (never `cf.*`, never bare) and so must its source — `useItemAttributes()` skips anything else, and `ItemAttributeCell` renders through `<Slot owner=…>`, so another plugin registering the same key draws nothing. **Host:** `lib/columns.ts` `columnCatalog(fields, projectId, attributes)` appends contributed columns (`ColumnDef.attribute`) between builtins and custom fields; `lib/item-attribute-data.ts` (`useItemAttributeValues` + `ItemAttributeContext`) asks each SHOWN attribute's source once per page of rows — stable ≤200-id batches (`useStableItemBatches`), one `useContributedQueries` request per (source, batch), shared by list, board and swimlanes — and `view.tsx` provides it around the surfaces, so no prop threads a plugin's data (`slaByItem` is gone). `ColumnCells`/`card-cells` draw a contributed value through `ItemAttributeCell` (no value = dash in lists, no cell on cards); the card designer palette lists them under **From plugins** and the preview renders each one's `sample`. **Degradation = a deleted custom field's:** a withdrawn attribute's id stays in `view.columns` / `card_layout`, the list skips it, the Columns editor shows `<id> (unavailable)`, the designer's stale note names it. SDK additions: `useContributedQueries` (many sources, the `useContributedQuery` identity and lifetime — an absent source asks nothing, the loader's withdrawal aborts in-flight reads), `QuerySource.refetchInterval`, `Slot owner`. Guards: `plugin-boundaries.test.mjs` (no SLA vocabulary on the host surfaces); proofs `web/scripts/browser-plugin-columns.mjs` (mocked API + real slas remote) and `sla-columns-proof.mjs` (real backend). | views, slas | — | — |
| `web` (frontend, `web/`) | React 19 + Vite SPA (TanStack Router + Query), built into `web/dist` and served by the API. Shell: `TopBar` + `PinsBar` above the sidebar/content split (`components/shell/`). Every work surface is a saved view (`routes/view.tsx` — board, list, planning, roadmap and plugin view types; projects seed plain Board/List/Planning views); the key-addressed issue page is `/issues/$itemKey` (spec 21, `routes/item-page.tsx`), with a peek panel (`components/items/IssuePanel.tsx`, `?peek=`) on list surfaces; My Work, Inbox, Timesheet, Reports and Settings (`routes/settings/`) are the other routes. The login page has no method tabs: the directory form shows only when the public LDAP capability is on, a local-account link always opens the password/MFA form, and SSO providers list below (RADD-1224). `lib/api.ts` is the typed client (`/api/v1`, cookie auth, 401 → /login); routes, query keys and API paths live in `lib/constants/`, queries in `lib/queries/`, invalidated by ENTITY (`lib/cache.ts`, below). Core plugins' UI is bundled from `<module>/ui/src` and registered at boot; optional plugins load as remotes (RADD-1373); both import only `@radd/plugin-sdk` (`web/packages/plugin-sdk`), which `web/scripts/plugin-boundaries.test.mjs` enforces — `docs/plugin-ui.md` is the contract. Styling is semantic tokens only (`web/src/index.css`). Browser proofs: `web/scripts/*.mjs` over CDP. | REST API contracts (`/openapi.json`) — no server imports | — | — |


## Audit trail, history & item links

The append-only `events` table is the **durable, attributable audit log** — every domain mutation already emits into it with `actor_id`. This wave makes it a first-class history + audit surface, plus adds external + version-control links:

- **Field-level diffs.** `items/changes.py:diff_item_reads` compares the pre-mutation and post-mutation `ItemRead`s; `item.updated` payloads now carry a `changes` `[{field, from, to, …}]` list (display values — state/assignee names, cf labels — resolved at write time so records stay accurate after renames/deletes) *alongside* the full snapshot (reporting still reads `state.id`). Computed in `items.service._finish` for every item mutation route (update, link add/remove — each hydrates a `before` snapshot); create + rank-only reorder carry none.
- **Per-item history.** `GET /items/{id}/history` merges the item's own change events with related events (comment/worklog/weblink/vcs) via `events.entity_activity`. `item.read`-scoped; internal-comment activity hidden without `comment.read_internal`. Related event types are wire strings in `items/history.py:RELATED_EVENT_TYPES` — **keep in sync** with the comments/timelogging/weblinks/vcs `*Event` enums.
- **Admin audit view.** `events.query_events` (filtered) backs the `audit` module. Indexes added in migration `f0f365b7e495`.
- **Link typeahead.** `GET /items/link-search?project_id&q&exclude_id` — items matching by title/number/key.
- **Frontend.** The issue view's Comments section became a tabbed **Activity** panel (Comments · History · Work log · Version control — `components/items/ActivityPanel`, `HistoryTab`, `WorklogTab`, `VcsPanel`); a **Related links** section (`RelatedLinksSection`) sits under Dependencies; the dependency add-row gained typeahead (`linkSearchQuery`); `/settings/audit` is the audit plugin's page (RADD-1364). New cache tags `Entity.webLink`/`vcsLink`; `itemHistoryQuery` is tagged with every entity that can appear in the feed (item/comment/worklog/webLink/vcsLink) so any of their mutations refresh it.

## Beyond the server modules

- **`sdk/`** — the extensions SDK (spec 44): an INDEPENDENT **Apache-2.0** uv project (`radd-sdk`) so private studio connectors are unambiguously fine. `RaddClient` (PAT-authed httpx wrapper + convenience calls), `Runner` (shotgunEvents ergonomics: drop `*.py` plugins exposing `register(reg)`/`reg.on(glob, cb)` in a dir; at-least-once dispatch off `GET /events`; per-plugin crash isolation; mtime hot-reload; atomic JSON offset checkpoint; `--from-start`), `radd-runner` CLI (flags + `RADD_URL/RADD_TOKEN/…` env), example plugin (`sla.breached` → label + comment), own pytest suite. ftrack/air-gapped-chat connectors are the canonical EXTERNAL extensions built on this — deliberately not in-core.
- **Packaging (spec 48)** — `Containerfile` (node builds `web/`, uv-locked server install, non-root, migrate-then-serve CMD), `compose.yaml` app service, **Helm chart** `deploy/helm/radd/` (web Deployment + optional worker split via `RADD_RUN_WORKERS`, pre-upgrade migration Job, Secret-driven env, PVC-or-S3 attachments, probes on `/instance/login-options`), `docs/deploy.md` (quickstarts, backup/restore, env reference). **Worker split**: the background loops (webhooks/automations/notify/search/sla/mailintake) gate their `on_startup` on `settings.run_workers`; realtime + storage init always run.
- **MFA/TOTP (spec 48)** — in `auth`: pure RFC-6238 `auth/totp.py` (RFC-vector-tested), `user_totp` table, `POST /auth/totp/setup|/confirm`, `DELETE /auth/totp` (code required), `GET /auth/totp`; `POST /auth/login` → 401 `totp_required` for enrolled accounts → `POST /auth/login/totp` (stateless second step, uniform 401). SSO/LDAP logins untouched (the IdP owns MFA).

## Not yet built

Every §8 pillar has a first implementation. Open gaps are tracked in the RADD project; per-area residue is under "Known simplifications" below.

## Connection rules

- Cross-module calls go through the other module's `service.py` public functions only. Models are private to their module (Alembic metadata aside) — **except the shared spine tables, below.**
- Every domain mutation calls `events.service.emit()` **in the same session/transaction** (the outbox guarantee). Event types are per-module `StrEnum`s (e.g. `ItemEvent.CREATED = "item.created"`).
- Custom fields must serialize **inline on the entity** in every representation (REST now; webhooks/MCP later). Never a side-channel endpoint. (See `research/architecture.md` — the Plane failure.)
- The `fields` registry is the single source of truth for dynamic schema. Consumers: item validation (now), OpenAPI (now, via marker scan), UI form config / MCP tools / webhook payload docs (later — same registry, no parallel definitions).
- **RBAC (spec 03, engine reworked by spec 06)** has one seam: routers (or the items/comments services, which resolve the project themselves) call `auth.authz.require(session, user, Permission.X, project=…|space_id=…)` → 403; `CurrentUser` → 401 when anonymous (the routes that accept the anonymous `Actor` are a frozen inventory, `tests/test_anonymous_surface.py`). Roles are DATA: a user's effective permissions on a project are the **union** of the roles granted to them there — directly, through a team they are on, or through a directory group that contains them (RADD-929 folded `project_members`/`project_teams` into `global_role_grants`; one table, one path) — plus the Baseline role every active user holds (RADD-773); instance admins hold every permission. `require()` returns the effective-permission frozenset (callers reuse it — items derives `has_manage`/`comment.read_internal` from it without a second lookup). Never re-implement checks in modules — call the seam in `auth/authz.py`. **A new action class is declared by the module that enforces it** (RADD-890): put a `PermissionSpec`/`CrudResourceSpec` on that module's `RaddPlugin` manifest, and add the matching member to `auth.types.Permission` (+ `_PROJECT_SCOPED`/`_SPACE_SCOPED` if it is not global) purely so call sites can name it — `tests/test_permission_ownership.py` fails if either half is missing. Nothing else in `auth/types.py` is touched: scope, prose, umbrella and CRUD family all come from the declaration.

**Spine tables.** See CLAUDE.md rule 1: the models of `auth`, `projects`, `items`, `workflow`, `teams` and `fields` may be imported for READS; every other module's models are off-limits, enforced by `tests/test_module_contracts.py`.

## Known load-order edges

`depends_on` orders plugin loading, but a handful of cross-module imports would cycle (or invert the declared order) at module scope, so they're **deferred into function bodies** — grep for `# deferred:`. The band-aids, mapped:

- `workflow/transitions.py` + `transitions_router.py` — defer `fields`, `items`, `timelogging`, `comments` lookups; those modules load after workflow. (Approvals is no longer one: its check arrives through the `TRANSITION_CHECK` socket, RADD-1383.)
- `cycles/service.py` — defers `teams.service`, `auth.authz`, `items.service`.
- `projects/router.py` — defers `settings.service` / `SettingKey` (settings depends on projects, so the import must stay one-way).
- `auth/authz.py` / `auth/grants.py` / `auth/router.py` — defer `teams.service` (teams loads after auth).
- `linktypes/service.py` defers `items.models.ItemLink`; `items/history.py` defers `comments.visibility` (a genuine import cycle).

## Frontend cache invalidation (the convention every module follows)

One domain object is cached under **many** TanStack Query keys (an item lives in
`["items"]`, `["viewItems"]`, `["slqItems"]`, `["item"]`, `["itemByKey"]`). If a
mutation forgets one — or a new module adds a new query that caches the same
entity — an edit made on one surface silently won't appear on the others until a
hard refresh. To make this **impossible to get wrong across modules**, invalidate
by ENTITY, never by key (`web/src/lib/cache.ts`):

- **Read queries self-declare what they cache:** every `useQuery`/`queryOptions`
  that returns a domain object sets `meta: entityMeta(Entity.item)` (list one tag
  per entity in its data — a query joining items+cycles tags both).
- **Mutations invalidate by entity:** `onSettled: () => invalidateEntities(queryClient, Entity.item)`
  — a predicate refreshes every query tagged with that entity, including ones
  added later by other modules. List every entity a mutation can change (e.g.
  commenting touches `Entity.comment` AND `Entity.item` for its `comment_count`).
- Optimistic writes still `setQueryData` the specific caches for an instant feel;
  the entity invalidation on settle is the safety net that keeps every surface live.
- **New module checklist:** add its object to the `Entity` map; tag its read
  queries with `entityMeta`; have its mutations call `invalidateEntities`. Then a
  future query anywhere that caches the same entity is refreshed automatically —
  neither side needs to know about the other. (Retrofitting the pre-existing
  non-item modules onto this is incremental; new modules start here.)

## Field grant semantics (spec 07 → spec 92)

Field visibility rides the generic access framework (spec 92): grants are `access_grants` rows on resource_type=`field` (resource_id = the field definition's id); builtin item fields take the same model on resource_type=`builtin_field` (resource_id = the field name). Subjects are user/team/role/group (RADD-832), scope global or per-project, accesses `read`|`write` with **write implying read** (a writer can see what they write) and deny rows beating allows (RADD-819). Checks resolve through `access/resolution.has_access` against the actor's `FieldAccessContext` (`fields/service.py` — `role_ids` incl. builtin-derived + team-granted roles, `team_ids`, transitive `group_ids`, `user_id`, `has_manage`):

- **No grants for an access → that access is default-open** (`default_open=True`): read for anyone with `item.read`, write for anyone with `item.update`.
- **Read grants exist** → the field's value is visible only if one of the actor's subjects holds a read OR write allow (and no deny matches).
- **Write grants exist** → writable only via a matching WRITE grant (read grants never confer write). Corollary: a field with only read grants stays write-open to `item.update` holders.
- `has_manage` short-circuits both checks, and since RADD-816 it means **instance admin** (the operator who administers the grant system), NOT `project.manage`: a project manager is denied like anyone else unless a grant names them. Grant management itself needs `field.manage` on the field's own scope (`_can_manage_field`).
- Grants are extra restrictions on top of action RBAC, never a bypass: without `item.read`/`item.update` on the project a grant is moot.
- History: migration `fa2747459c4b` converted the original min-role ladder into `field_permissions` rows; the spec-92 migration `6fc5f7c71481` moved those into `access_grants` and dropped the table.

## SLQ — the Radd Query language (spec 10)

`items/slq/` (`lexer.py` → `parser.py` AST → `compiler.py` SQLAlchemy; op tables in `catalog.py`). `GET /items?q=…` takes it; saved views store it (`views.query`). The grammar is FROZEN — frontends render their cheat sheet from it:

```
query     := [expr] [ORDER BY order (, order)*]
expr      := term ((AND|OR) term)*        # AND binds tighter than OR
term      := [NOT] (comparison | '(' expr ')')
comparison:= field op value | field [NOT] IN '(' value (',' value)* ')' | field IS [NOT] EMPTY
op        := = | != | ~ | > | < | >= | <=
value     := bareword | 'single' | "double" quoted | number | YYYY-MM-DD | today[±Nd|Nw] | me | none
order     := field [ASC|DESC]
```

Keywords case-insensitive; field names case-sensitive; quoted values are always literal (never sentinels/keywords). Builtin fields: `project` (key), `state` (name), `category`, `kind`, `priority`, `assignee` (email | `me` | `none`), `team` (name | `none`), `label` (`=` has, `!=` lacks, `IN` has any, `IS EMPTY` unlabeled), `title` (`~` case-insensitive contains / `=`), `key` (`= TD-12`), `parent` (key | `none`), `number`, `created`/`updated` (date comparisons with whole-day windows: `= d` means within the day, `> d` strictly after it), and the **planning fields (spec 14)**: `cycle` (`= name` \| `= none` \| `!=` \| `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; matches `cycle_id` by cycle NAME via subquery, workspace-unscoped like `state`), `release` (`= version` \| `= none` \| `!=` \| `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; by release VERSION), `blocks`/`blocked` (`IS [NOT] EMPTY` = has an outgoing/incoming `blocks` link — an EXISTS over `item_links`; `= <key>` = links to/blocked-by that item; `=` only, no `!=`/`IN`), `start`/`target` (plain Date-column comparisons — no whole-day windowing — `= != > < >= <=` + `IS [NOT] EMPTY`; **not sortable**), `flagged` (**spec 24** — first-class boolean: `= true` \| `= false`, sortable, autocompletes true/false, other values → 422), `starred` (**spec 24** — the requesting user's personal star: `= true` \| `= false`, an EXISTS over item_stars scoped to `current_user_id`), `rank` (**spec 24** — manual sort key, **`ORDER BY rank` only**: no compare ops, excluded from filter-field autocomplete) — plus **every custom field by bare registry key** (select/text/url: `= != ~ IN`; user: `= != IN`; multi_select: containment — `=` contains, `!=` lacks, `IN` contains any; number/duration/date: comparisons; boolean: `= true|false`; all cf support `IS [NOT] EMPTY`). Unknown field or type-invalid op → 422 `{detail, position}` (character offset), with a one-edit did-you-mean on unknown fields. Compilation is subquery-based (state/project/user/team lookups, EXISTS for labels, JSONB ops for cf — `@>` for equality, `->> ::cast` for ranges); labels resolve name→id through the labels service; cf typing uses the same key→definition map as the `cf` param (project registry when `project_id` is given, instance-wide otherwise). `ORDER BY` sortables: `created`, `updated`, `number`, `title`, `priority`/`kind` (enum rank), sortable cf types (cast per type); `created DESC` always appended as tiebreak/default. `!=`/`NOT IN` on a nullable to-one relation (assignee/reporter/team/type/cycle/release, the ancestors, the worklog dialect's issue/project/category) are the COMPLEMENT of the positive form (RADD-1139, `helpers.polarity(nullable=True)` compiles `IS NOT TRUE` rather than `NOT`): items with the relation unset DO match — `assignee != me` includes the unassigned, `= x` and `!= x` partition the rows — while `!= none` still means "set" (its positive form is `IS NULL`); scalar-cf `!=` means "has a different value" (missing excluded, unchanged); multi_select/label `!=` means "lacks" (missing included). **Relative dates (spec 69):** every date position (`created`/`updated`/`start`/`target` + date custom fields) also accepts bare `today`, `today+3d`, `today-2w` (units `d`/`w`; a QUOTED 'today' stays literal, like the me/none sentinels), resolved at COMPILE time against the server date (`helpers.relative_date`, pure with injectable `today`) — a saved view using `today` shows results as-of query time; `today` leads the date-field suggest values (tier-sentinel, bare insert). **Ancestor fields (spec 83, `items/slq/ancestors.py`):** `epic` = the NEAREST EPIC ANCESTOR (an issue's parent, a subtask's grandparent — one aliased P/G self-join chain per condition deriving `CASE WHEN P.kind='epic' THEN P.id WHEN G.kind='epic' THEN G.id END` as a correlated scalar subquery; NULL for epics themselves and parentless items, so `epic IS EMPTY` covers both), `parent` stays the direct parent; the bare forms take `= key` / `!= key` / `IN (key, none)` / `IS [NOT] EMPTY` with keys resolved at COMPILE time through the spec-68 alias-aware `find_item_by_key` (a `compile_query` pre-pass like label resolution; unknown key → positioned 422 — a change from the old match-nothing `parent = key` subquery, so moved items keep matching under their pre-move keys). Sub-fields `epic.state`/`epic.category`/`epic.assignee`/`epic.priority` (+ the `parent.*` twins) mirror the item-level value semantics (state NAMES case-INsensitive here per the spec; category/priority enum-validated with positioned 422s; assignee `me`/`none`/email via the shared `builtins.user_match` helper) and compile to `<ancestor id> IN (SELECT id FROM work_items WHERE …)` — correlated comparisons, never joins — so OR/NOT hold, and the negative forms read plainly (RADD-1139: `epic.category != done` includes work under no epic — the complement of `= done`; positive predicates, `epic.assignee IS EMPTY` included, never match an item without the ancestor); cross-project ancestors (spec 80) match naturally since traversal is by id. Filter-only: none are sortable (`ORDER BY epic.state` → the standard not-sortable 422); suggest completes all nine field names and reuses the item-level value sources (state names, categories, users incl. `me`/`none`, priorities; bare `epic`/`parent` offer `none` + item keys).

## Saved views ↔ items (specs 08 + 10)

Planning (RADD-1202, [guide](planning-pagination.md)) has active/upcoming/draft sprint sections, a separate rescheduling section for open historical work, an independently paged/searched/sorted backlog and explicitly opened per-sprint history (`lib/planning-query.ts`, `components/views/PlanningControls.tsx`); `cycle.status` delegates to the cycle directory's lifecycle expression. Visibility/history settings live in Display, each section has a server-backed title filter (`lib/usePlanningSectionSearch.ts`, RADD-1207), and filters keep section stats with independent matching counts. The large-view loading design and its measurements: `research/large-views-2026-09/`.

A saved view carries SLQ (`query`) and its `ViewRead.query_string` is the ready-made `GET /items?` composition — `q=<urlencoded>` (omitted when the query is empty) plus `project_id=<id>` for project-scoped views. The spec-08 structured params (`state_id`, `category`, `kind`, `priority`, `assignee_id`/`team_id` incl. literal `none`, `label`, `cf=k:v`) all remain on `GET /items` and AND with `q`: within one param values OR, across params AND. Malformed values → 422. Performance note: cf equality uses JSONB containment (`custom_fields @> …`) — sequential scan today; expression/GIN indexes come later keyed off the field registry's `indexed` flag. Migration `6afdeaf4f62d` converted every stored `ViewFilters` document to equivalent SLQ (ids resolved to names/emails; `none` → `IS EMPTY`; unresolvable references dropped clause-wise, logged) and dropped the `filters` column.

## Known simplifications (fix when they matter)

- OpenAPI is rebuilt per request; the field-schema cache refreshes in-transaction, so a rolled-back field create can leave it one step ahead until the next mutation.
- Default-state seeding runs in the `project.created` hook, which carries no actor, so those `state.created` events are actor-less.
- Expired sessions are never pruned; PAT `last_used_at` is throttled (`RADD_TOKEN_LAST_USED_THROTTLE_SECONDS`), so it is approximate.
- A state's `is_default` cannot be moved; item labels replace wholesale on PATCH.
- A custom-field key shadowed by a builtin name or an SLQ keyword is unreachable in queries.
- LDAP is AD-flavoured only (UPN bind, `sAMAccountName`, AD's transitive group rule).
- The extensions SDK polls (2 s), one offset per runner process.
- Realtime, webhooks and automations run in-process on a single replica; multi-replica needs a shared bus and `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`.
- Internal-comment bodies are not search-indexed; search has no typo tolerance.
- Reports recompute from the event log on every request (no read model).

## Remediation and ownership waves

How the September 2026 audit (RADD-1112/1115) was remediated: `research/audit-2026-09-09/REMEDIATION.md`. The large-view performance work (RADD-1204–1217): `research/large-views-2026-09/`. The plugin-ownership wave (RADD-1343): `research/plugin-isolation/README.md`. The module rows above describe the result.
